## 🔤 P

**PAINSTAKING** *(adjective)* | অত্যন্ত যত্নশীল, পরিশ্রমী
Done with or employing great care and thoroughness; meticulous.
✅ meticulous, careful, thorough, diligent, scrupulous, precise, exact, detailed
❌ careless, sloppy, hasty, negligent, rushed, inattentive, lazy
📝 The **painstaking** restoration of the ancient manuscript took five years.

**PANDEMIC** *(noun/adjective)* | বিশ্বব্যাপী মহামারী
A disease prevalent over a whole country or the world; very widespread.
✅ epidemic, widespread, universal, global, prevalent, pervasive, rampant
❌ local, contained, limited, isolated, controlled, rare, confined
📝 The **pandemic** brought economies across the world to a standstill.

**PARCHED** *(adjective)* | শুষ্ক, তৃষ্ণার্ত, পানিশূন্য
Dried out with heat; extremely thirsty; dry and shriveled.
✅ dry, arid, dehydrated, scorched, shriveled, withered, thirsty, desiccated
❌ moist, damp, wet, hydrated, saturated, lush, watered
📝 Survivors of the desert crossing were completely **parched** and dehydrated.

**PASSIVE** *(adjective)* | নিষ্ক্রিয়, নিরামিষ, আত্মসমর্পণকারী
Accepting what happens without active response; submissive and yielding.
✅ inactive, submissive, yielding, compliant, docile, unresisting, unassertive
❌ active, assertive, aggressive, proactive, dominant, forceful, combative
📝 A **passive** stance in the face of injustice is itself a form of complicity.

**PECULIAR** *(adjective)* | অদ্ভুত, বিচিত্র, স্বতন্ত্র
Strange or odd; unusual; belonging exclusively to a particular place or person.
✅ strange, unusual, bizarre, odd, weird, eccentric, unique, quirky, curious
❌ ordinary, normal, typical, common, familiar, conventional, standard
📝 There was a **peculiar** smell coming from the old cupboard in the corner.

**PEJORATIVE** *(adjective)* | অবমাননামূলক, নিন্দামূলক, হেয়কর
Expressing contempt or disapproval; insulting or degrading in tone.
✅ derogatory, insulting, demeaning, belittling, disparaging, critical, dismissive
❌ complimentary, praising, flattering, admiring, respectful, uplifting
📝 The use of **pejorative** language in the report was widely condemned.

**PENURY** *(noun)* | চরম দারিদ্র্য, অভাব-অনটন
Extreme poverty; being very poor; great lack of money or resources.
✅ poverty, destitution, need, deprivation, hardship, want, paucity, destitution
❌ wealth, affluence, prosperity, abundance, luxury, comfort, opulence
📝 After the business failed, the family lived in **penury** for several years.

**PERFIDY** *(noun)* | বিশ্বাসঘাতকতা, প্রতারণা, ধোঁকা
Deceitfulness and untrustworthiness; the action of betraying someone's trust.
✅ treachery, betrayal, deceit, disloyalty, faithlessness, duplicity, treason
❌ loyalty, fidelity, faithfulness, honesty, integrity, trustworthiness, devotion
📝 The general's **perfidy** shocked everyone who had trusted him for years.

**PERILOUS** *(adjective)* | বিপজ্জনক, ঝুঁকিপূর্ণ, ভয়াবহ
Full of danger or risk; hazardous and potentially very harmful.
✅ dangerous, hazardous, risky, treacherous, unsafe, threatening, dire, deadly
❌ safe, secure, harmless, protected, risk-free, stable, benign
📝 The **perilous** sea voyage claimed the lives of three of the twelve sailors.

**PERNICIOUS** *(adjective)* | ক্ষতিকর, বিষাক্ত, মারাত্মক
Having a harmful effect, especially in a gradual or subtle way; deadly.
✅ harmful, damaging, destructive, deadly, toxic, injurious, malignant, lethal
❌ harmless, beneficial, helpful, safe, constructive, healing, good
📝 The **pernicious** habit of skipping sleep has long-term health consequences.

**PERSISTENT** *(adjective)* | অদম্য, অনবরত, জেদী
Continuing firmly despite difficulty; refusing to give up; tenacious.
✅ determined, relentless, persevering, unyielding, tenacious, stubborn, steadfast
❌ yielding, flexible, giving up, irresolute, half-hearted, stopping, quitting
📝 Her **persistent** job applications finally landed her an interview at her dream company.

**PERSUADE / PERSUASION** *(verb/noun)* | মানানো, বোঝানো / মানানোর কৌশল
To cause someone to do or believe something through reasoning or charm.
✅ convince, influence, cajole, coax, induce, win over, sway, tempt
❌ dissuade, discourage, deter, prevent, stop, repel, put off
📝 It took him an entire evening to **persuade** her to give the project one more try.

**PERTINENT** *(adjective)* | প্রাসঙ্গিক, বিষয়সংক্রান্ত
Relevant or applicable to a particular matter; to the point.
✅ relevant, applicable, related, fitting, apt, appropriate, germane, suitable
❌ irrelevant, unrelated, beside the point, immaterial, inapplicable, off-topic
📝 Please make sure all comments are **pertinent** to the topic being discussed.

**PERVADE / PERVASIVE** *(verb/adjective)* | ছড়িয়ে পড়া / সর্বব্যাপী
To spread through every part of something; (pervasive) existing everywhere.
✅ permeate, penetrate, fill, spread, saturate, diffuse, omnipresent, widespread
❌ contain, limit, concentrate, restrict, hold back, localize
📝 A spirit of optimism **pervaded** the entire country after the election results.

**PHILANTHROPY** *(noun)* | মানবপ্রেম, সমাজসেবা, দান-দক্ষিণা
The desire to promote the welfare of others through donations and generosity.
✅ charity, benevolence, generosity, humanitarianism, altruism, goodwill, giving
❌ selfishness, miserliness, greed, cruelty, misanthropy, avarice
📝 Her **philanthropy** funded hospitals, libraries, and schools in three countries.

**PHLEGMATIC** *(adjective)* | অলস, শান্তস্বভাবী, ঠান্ডা মাথার
Having an unemotional, calm, and sluggish temperament; not easily excited.
✅ calm, unemotional, sluggish, apathetic, impassive, unexcitable, stoic
❌ emotional, passionate, excitable, energetic, animated, enthusiastic, lively
📝 He approached every crisis with the same **phlegmatic** calm that never wavered.

**PITHY** *(adjective)* | সংক্ষিপ্ত ও অর্থবহ, সারগর্ভ
Concise and forcefully expressive; brief but full of meaning and substance.
✅ concise, brief, succinct, terse, meaningful, pointed, laconic, compact
❌ verbose, long-winded, wordy, vague, empty, meaningless, rambling
📝 The professor was famous for delivering **pithy** observations students never forgot.

**PLACID** *(adjective)* | শান্ত, নিবিড়, স্থির, প্রশান্ত
Not easily upset or excited; calm and gentle; peaceful and tranquil.
✅ calm, serene, tranquil, peaceful, gentle, composed, unruffled, still
❌ agitated, anxious, troubled, excited, stormy, restless, turbulent
📝 The **placid** lake was perfect for an early morning canoe ride.

**PLAUDIT** *(noun)* | প্রশংসা, তারিফ, করতালি
Praise and approval; enthusiastic applause or acclaim from an audience.
✅ praise, applause, acclaim, approval, commendation, accolade, tribute, ovation
❌ criticism, disapproval, booing, condemnation, blame, censure
📝 The young pianist received warm **plaudits** after her debut performance.

**PLAUSIBLE** *(adjective)* | যুক্তিসংগত, বিশ্বাসযোগ্য, সম্ভাব্য
Seeming reasonable or probable; credible, believable, and convincing.
✅ believable, reasonable, credible, probable, convincing, likely, logical, possible
❌ implausible, unlikely, incredible, unbelievable, far-fetched, absurd
📝 The alibi was **plausible** enough that investigators had to take it seriously.

**PRECARIOUS** *(adjective)* | অনিশ্চিত, ঝুঁকিপূর্ণ, অস্থির
Not securely held or in position; uncertain and dependent on chance.
✅ unstable, dangerous, risky, uncertain, insecure, shaky, vulnerable, doubtful
❌ stable, secure, safe, certain, firm, reliable, guaranteed
📝 He lived in **precarious** financial conditions for most of his adult life.

**PREPOSTEROUS** *(adjective)* | হাস্যকর, অযৌক্তিক, অসম্ভব
Contrary to reason or common sense; utterly absurd or ridiculous.
✅ absurd, ridiculous, outrageous, nonsensical, ludicrous, irrational, bizarre
❌ reasonable, sensible, logical, rational, plausible, sound, justified
📝 The suggestion that the Earth is flat is completely **preposterous**.

**PRETENTIOUS** *(adjective)* | ভান করা, দেখানোর জন্য, কৃত্রিম
Attempting to impress by affecting greater importance or merit than one has.
✅ showy, ostentatious, affected, pompous, self-important, conceited, grandiose
❌ modest, genuine, humble, sincere, unpretentious, down-to-earth, authentic
📝 The **pretentious** review was more about showing off than actually critiquing the film.

**PROCURE** *(verb)* | সংগ্রহ করা, যোগাড় করা
To obtain something, especially with care or effort; to get or acquire.
✅ obtain, acquire, get, secure, gather, source, find, purchase
❌ lose, give away, discard, surrender, fail to get, relinquish
📝 It was difficult to **procure** fresh vegetables in such a remote location.

**PROFOUND** *(adjective)* | গভীর, তীব্র, গুরুত্বপূর্ণ
Very great or intense; having deep insight or understanding; far-reaching.
✅ deep, intense, serious, thorough, great, significant, meaningful, comprehensive
❌ shallow, superficial, minor, trivial, unimportant, slight, basic
📝 The book had a **profound** impact on how she thought about justice.

**PRUDENT** *(adjective)* | বিচক্ষণ, সুচিন্তিত, সতর্ক
Acting with or showing care and thought for the future; wise in judgment.
✅ wise, careful, sensible, thoughtful, cautious, judicious, farsighted, shrewd
❌ reckless, imprudent, careless, foolish, impulsive, unwise, rash
📝 It would be **prudent** to save at least three months of expenses as an emergency fund.

**PUNCTILIOUS** *(adjective)* | অতিসতর্ক, নিখুঁত নিয়ম মেনে চলা
Showing great attention to detail or correct behaviour; very precise.
✅ meticulous, precise, exacting, strict, thorough, scrupulous, careful, exact
❌ careless, sloppy, lazy, negligent, inattentive, casual, lax
📝 Her **punctilious** approach to grammar made her the best proofreader in the team.

## 🔤 Q

**QUASH** *(verb)* | দমন করা, বাতিল করা, চাপা দেওয়া
To reject or void a decision; to suppress or put an end to something forcefully.
✅ suppress, crush, squash, quell, overturn, nullify, cancel, stop
❌ allow, support, encourage, confirm, uphold, approve, permit
📝 The appeal court moved to **quash** the original guilty verdict.

**QUELL** *(verb)* | দমন করা, শান্ত করা
To put an end to a rebellion or other disorder; to suppress or subdue.
✅ suppress, subdue, crush, quash, calm, silence, put down, overcome
❌ incite, provoke, encourage, inflame, stir up, arouse
📝 Police were called to **quell** the disturbance outside the stadium.

**QUIXOTIC** *(adjective)* | অবাস্তব, আদর্শবাদী কিন্তু অপ্রাসঙ্গিক
Extremely idealistic and unrealistic; impractically romantic; fanciful.
✅ idealistic, impractical, unrealistic, romantic, fanciful, utopian, dreamy
❌ realistic, practical, pragmatic, grounded, sensible, achievable
📝 His **quixotic** plan to end world hunger with one invention never went anywhere.

## 🔤 R

**RANCOROUS** *(adjective)* | বিদ্বেষপূর্ণ, তিক্ত, শত্রুভাবাপন্ন
Having or showing bitter, long-lasting resentment or ill will; deeply hostile.
✅ bitter, resentful, hostile, acrimonious, hateful, vengeful, vindictive, malicious
❌ kind, forgiving, friendly, amicable, benevolent, peaceful, generous
📝 The **rancorous** dispute tore the organization apart over several bitter years.

**RATIFY** *(verb)* | অনুমোদন করা, আনুষ্ঠানিকভাবে স্বীকৃতি দেওয়া
To give formal consent to make something officially valid; to approve.
✅ approve, confirm, endorse, validate, sanction, authorize, pass, agree to
❌ reject, veto, deny, disapprove, annul, overturn, cancel
📝 Parliament voted to **ratify** the trade agreement before the deadline.

**RECKLESS** *(adjective)* | বেপরোয়া, হঠকারী, দায়িত্বহীন
Without thinking about the danger or consequences of one's actions; hasty.
✅ careless, hasty, irresponsible, impetuous, impulsive, rash, heedless, foolhardy
❌ careful, cautious, prudent, thoughtful, responsible, deliberate, considered
📝 **Reckless** behaviour on construction sites puts everyone's life at risk.

**RECIPROCATE** *(verb)* | পারস্পরিক আদান-প্রদান করা, প্রতিদান দেওয়া
To respond to a gesture or action by making a corresponding one in return.
✅ return, exchange, match, respond in kind, give back, repay, mirror
❌ ignore, refuse, reject, withhold, keep, take without giving
📝 He hoped she would **reciprocate** his kindness when he needed help.

**RECONCILE** *(verb)* | মিটমাট করা, মেলানো
To restore friendly relations between people; to make compatible or consistent.
✅ settle, resolve, harmonize, reunite, patch up, make peace, balance
❌ divide, separate, worsen, conflict, clash, disagree, alienate
📝 The mediator worked for weeks to **reconcile** the feuding business partners.

**RECTIFY** *(verb)* | সংশোধন করা, ঠিক করা
To put something right; to correct or remedy a mistake or problem.
✅ correct, fix, repair, remedy, resolve, sort out, amend, put right
❌ worsen, damage, break, ignore, maintain error, repeat, overlook
📝 The company promised to **rectify** the error in the billing system within 24 hours.

**REDUNDANT** *(adjective)* | অপ্রয়োজনীয়, অতিরিক্ত, বাহুল্যপূর্ণ
Not needed or useful; superfluous; using more words than necessary.
✅ unnecessary, superfluous, extra, excessive, surplus, repetitive, extraneous
❌ necessary, essential, needed, important, vital, required, useful
📝 With automation rising, many jobs are becoming **redundant** in manufacturing.

**RELENTLESS** *(adjective)* | অদম্য, অবিরাম, নিষ্ঠুর
Unceasingly intense and not yielding; never stopping; harsh and without mercy.
✅ persistent, unyielding, unstoppable, ceaseless, merciless, unremitting, fierce
❌ mild, gentle, stopping, yielding, merciful, forgiving, lenient
📝 The team faced **relentless** pressure from the opposition throughout the match.

**RELUCTANT / RELUCTANCE** *(adjective/noun)* | অনিচ্ছুক / অনিচ্ছা
Not willing to do something and therefore slow to do it; hesitant.
✅ unwilling, hesitant, disinclined, unenthusiastic, loath, resistant, averse
❌ willing, eager, enthusiastic, happy to do, ready, keen, inclined
📝 She was **reluctant** to take the promotion because it meant moving cities.

**REMORSE** *(noun)* | অনুশোচনা, অনুতাপ
Deep regret or guilt for a wrong committed; a feeling of sorrow over past actions.
✅ guilt, regret, repentance, sorrow, contrition, shame, anguish, compunction
❌ pride, satisfaction, indifference, contentment, justification, arrogance
📝 He was filled with **remorse** after realizing how much his words had hurt her.

**RENOUNCE** *(verb)* | ত্যাগ করা, প্রত্যাখ্যান করা
To formally declare one's abandonment of a claim, right, or possession.
✅ abandon, give up, reject, disown, relinquish, forgo, surrender, repudiate
❌ claim, keep, maintain, hold, assert, pursue, retain, embrace
📝 She chose to **renounce** her inheritance to prove her independence.

**REPRIMAND** *(verb/noun)* | তিরস্কার করা, ধমক দেওয়া, ভর্ৎসনা করা
A formal expression of disapproval; to formally rebuke or scold someone.
✅ rebuke, scold, admonish, criticize, censure, chide, reprove, tell off
❌ praise, compliment, commend, approve, encourage, reward, congratulate
📝 The manager gave him a sharp **reprimand** for sharing confidential information.

**REPROBATE** *(noun/adjective)* | নীতিহীন, দুর্নীতিগ্রস্ত, অধম মানুষ
An unprincipled person; someone morally corrupt or depraved.
✅ scoundrel, villain, wretch, degenerate, rogue, miscreant, sinner
❌ saint, virtuous person, honorable person, moral, upright
📝 The old miser was known as the town **reprobate** for his many corrupt dealings.

**RESILIENCE** *(noun)* | ঘুরে দাঁড়ানোর শক্তি, স্থিতিস্থাপকতা
The capacity to recover quickly from difficulties; mental and emotional toughness.
✅ toughness, strength, perseverance, adaptability, endurance, stamina, flexibility
❌ weakness, fragility, vulnerability, inability, rigidity, collapse
📝 The community's **resilience** after the earthquake impressed rescue workers worldwide.

**RETICENT** *(adjective)* | কম কথার, মুখচোরা, চুপচাপ
Not revealing one's thoughts or feelings readily; reserved and quiet.
✅ reserved, quiet, secretive, shy, silent, withdrawn, uncommunicative, guarded
❌ talkative, open, outgoing, expressive, forthcoming, loquacious, candid
📝 She was **reticent** about her past and rarely discussed it with anyone.

**REVERENCE** *(noun)* | শ্রদ্ধা, সমীহ, ভক্তি
Deep respect and admiration; a feeling of awe and veneration.
✅ respect, admiration, awe, honor, veneration, esteem, deference, worship
❌ disrespect, contempt, disdain, irreverence, scorn, mockery
📝 The students stood in **reverence** as the founding principal entered the hall.

**REVERE** *(verb)* | শ্রদ্ধা করা, সম্মান করা
To feel deep respect or admiration for someone; to venerate.
✅ respect, admire, honor, esteem, venerate, worship, look up to, adore
❌ disrespect, despise, mock, dishonor, ridicule, scorn, belittle
📝 The scientist was universally **revered** for her decades of breakthrough research.

**RHETORIC / RHETORICAL** *(noun/adjective)* | বাকশিল্প, বাগাড়ম্বর
The art of effective or persuasive speaking; language designed to have a persuasive effect.
✅ oratory, persuasion, eloquence, public speaking, discourse, language
❌ silence, plain speech, candor, direct talk, bluntness
📝 His speech was full of **rhetoric** but short on concrete policy proposals.

**RIDICULOUS** *(adjective)* | হাস্যকর, অযৌক্তিক, বেতুকা
Deserving or inviting mockery or derision; absurd; very silly.
✅ absurd, ludicrous, preposterous, laughable, nonsensical, comical, silly
❌ reasonable, sensible, logical, serious, valid, justified, rational
📝 The idea sounded **ridiculous** at first, but it eventually proved to be brilliant.

**RUTHLESS** *(adjective)* | নির্দয়, নিষ্ঠুর, দয়াহীন
Having no pity or compassion; mercilessly harsh and cruel.
✅ merciless, cruel, heartless, callous, pitiless, brutal, relentless, unfeeling
❌ merciful, kind, compassionate, gentle, humane, forgiving, caring
📝 The **ruthless** competitor used every available tactic to eliminate rivals.

## 🔤 Z

**ZENITH** *(noun)* | শীর্ষবিন্দু, সর্বোচ্চ অবস্থা
The highest point reached by a celestial body; the peak of success or power.
✅ peak, pinnacle, apex, summit, top, height, acme, climax, crest
❌ nadir, lowest point, base, bottom, trough, depth, decline
📝 She was at the absolute **zenith** of her career when the award was announced.

# 📚 SUPPLEMENTARY WORDS — Additional from Transcript
## Words not yet covered in Parts 1–7

---

## 🔤 A (Additional)

**ABHOR** *(verb)* | ঘৃণা করা, অত্যন্ত অপছন্দ করা
To regard with disgust and hatred; to detest intensely.
✅ detest, loathe, despise, hate, execrate, find repulsive
❌ love, adore, admire, like, cherish, appreciate
📝 She **abhors** any form of dishonesty in personal or professional life.

**ABSCOND** *(verb)* | পালিয়ে যাওয়া, চুরি করে পালানো
To leave hurriedly and secretly, typically to avoid detection or arrest.
✅ flee, escape, run away, bolt, disappear, slip away, vanish
❌ stay, remain, appear, return, face, surrender, come back
📝 The accountant **absconded** with company funds before anyone noticed.

**ABSTEMIOUS** *(adjective)* | সংযমী, মিতাহারী
Not allowing oneself to have much food, drink, or pleasure; restrained.
✅ temperate, restrained, moderate, self-denying, austere, frugal, disciplined
❌ indulgent, excessive, greedy, gluttonous, self-indulgent, intemperate
📝 He was known for his **abstemious** habits — one small meal a day.

**ABSTAIN** *(verb)* | বিরত থাকা, পরহেজ করা
To restrain oneself from doing or enjoying something; to refrain.
✅ refrain, desist, hold back, forgo, avoid, withhold, give up
❌ indulge, partake, participate, engage, consume, enjoy
📝 She chose to **abstain** from voting because she disagreed with all candidates.

**ABSTRACT** *(adjective/noun)* | বিমূর্ত, তাত্ত্বিক, সারসংক্ষেপ
Existing in thought or as an idea but not having a physical reality; theoretical.
✅ conceptual, theoretical, non-concrete, intangible, notional, philosophical
❌ concrete, specific, real, tangible, physical, definite, practical
📝 The professor's lecture was so **abstract** that few students could follow it.

**ACCELERATE** *(verb)* | ত্বরান্বিত করা, গতি বাড়ানো
To begin to move more quickly; to cause something to happen sooner.
✅ speed up, quicken, hasten, hurry, increase, advance, expedite
❌ slow down, decelerate, delay, hinder, brake, retard, impede
📝 Investment in infrastructure will **accelerate** economic development significantly.

**ACCLAIM** *(verb/noun)* | প্রশংসা করা, সমাদর করা
Enthusiastic and public praise; to praise publicly and enthusiastically.
✅ praise, applaud, cheer, commend, celebrate, honour, laud, extol
❌ criticize, condemn, boo, censure, denounce, ignore, dismiss
📝 The novel received immediate critical **acclaim** after its release.

**ACCOMMODATE** *(verb)* | ব্যবস্থা করা, মিটমাট করা
To provide lodging or space for; to adapt to the needs of someone.
✅ house, provide for, adapt, adjust, hold, fit, contain, cater for
❌ reject, exclude, refuse, deny, expel, turn away, ignore
📝 The hotel can **accommodate** up to three hundred guests at full capacity.

**ACCOMPLISH** *(verb)* | সম্পন্ন করা, অর্জন করা
To achieve or complete something successfully; to carry out a task.
✅ achieve, complete, fulfill, attain, finish, carry out, realize, execute
❌ fail, abandon, leave unfinished, give up, neglect, miss
📝 She managed to **accomplish** more in one month than others do in a year.

**ACCOLADE** *(noun)* | সম্মান, পুরস্কার, প্রশংসা
An award or privilege granted as recognition of excellence; special praise.
✅ award, honor, tribute, prize, recognition, commendation, distinction
❌ criticism, punishment, condemnation, censure, dishonor, rebuke
📝 Winning the national award was the highest **accolade** she had ever received.

**ACQUIESCE** *(verb)* | মেনে নেওয়া, রাজি হওয়া নীরবে
To accept something reluctantly but without protest; to comply passively.
✅ agree, consent, comply, accept, submit, yield, go along with, concede
❌ resist, oppose, object, refuse, protest, reject, dispute, fight
📝 Though unhappy, he chose to **acquiesce** to the manager's final decision.

**ACUTE** *(adjective)* | তীব্র, তীক্ষ্ণ, তীব্র বুদ্ধিমান
(Of an unpleasant situation) very severe; having a sharp, perceptive mind.
✅ severe, intense, sharp, keen, perceptive, serious, critical, penetrating
❌ mild, dull, moderate, slight, blunt, unimportant, minor, trivial
📝 He has an **acute** sense of observation that misses nothing.

**ADHERE** *(verb)* | মেনে চলা, লেগে থাকা
To stick firmly to a surface; to believe in and follow the practices of.
✅ stick, cling, follow, abide by, comply with, observe, maintain
❌ abandon, ignore, violate, defy, disobey, break, detach
📝 All employees must **adhere** to the company's code of conduct at all times.

**ADORN** *(verb)* | সাজানো, সুসজ্জিত করা
To make more attractive; to decorate with beautiful things.
✅ decorate, embellish, beautify, ornament, enhance, grace, garnish
❌ disfigure, strip, simplify, spoil, bare, uglify, remove
📝 Flowers were used to **adorn** every table at the wedding reception.

**ADVERSARY** *(noun)* | প্রতিপক্ষ, শত্রু, প্রতিদ্বন্দ্বী
One's opponent in a contest, conflict, or dispute; an enemy.
✅ opponent, enemy, rival, foe, antagonist, competitor, nemesis
❌ ally, friend, supporter, companion, partner, helper, teammate
📝 He studied his **adversary's** tactics carefully before the championship match.

**AFFLICT** *(verb)* | কষ্ট দেওয়া, যন্ত্রণা দেওয়া
To cause pain, suffering, or distress to; to trouble greatly.
✅ trouble, distress, torment, burden, pain, plague, beset, harm
❌ comfort, relieve, soothe, heal, help, cure, ease, benefit
📝 The disease continues to **afflict** millions of people across three continents.

**AGGRAVATE** *(verb)* | আরও খারাপ করা, বিরক্ত করা
To make a problem, injury, or offence worse or more serious; to annoy.
✅ worsen, intensify, exacerbate, irritate, annoy, inflame, compound
❌ improve, soothe, alleviate, mitigate, calm, ease, help, reduce
📝 Scratching the wound will only **aggravate** the infection further.

**ALLEVIATE** *(verb)* | উপশম করা, কমানো, হালকা করা
To make something less severe; to reduce pain, suffering, or difficulty.
✅ ease, reduce, lessen, relieve, mitigate, diminish, soothe, lighten
❌ worsen, aggravate, intensify, increase, magnify, heighten, amplify
📝 Meditation can help to **alleviate** the symptoms of chronic stress.

**ALLUDE** *(verb)* | ইঙ্গিত করা, উল্লেখ করা পরোক্ষে
To suggest or call attention to indirectly; to make a passing reference.
✅ hint, suggest, refer, imply, insinuate, intimate, indicate
❌ state directly, declare, specify, name outright, announce, assert
📝 He **alluded** to his difficult past without ever saying it directly.

**AMBIVALENT** *(adjective)* | দ্বিধাগ্রস্ত, দুই মনের
Having mixed feelings or contradictory ideas about something; uncertain.
✅ uncertain, undecided, torn, conflicted, hesitant, equivocal, irresolute
❌ certain, decisive, clear, resolved, committed, definite, sure
📝 She felt **ambivalent** about the promotion — excited but also anxious.

**AMELIORATE** *(verb)* | উন্নত করা, ভালো করা
To make something bad or unsatisfactory better; to improve conditions.
✅ improve, enhance, better, upgrade, relieve, help, remedy, reform
❌ worsen, damage, harm, deteriorate, neglect, impair, degrade
📝 New policies were introduced to **ameliorate** working conditions for all staff.

**AMEND** *(verb)* | সংশোধন করা, পরিবর্তন করা
To make minor changes to a text, law, or document to correct or improve it.
✅ correct, revise, modify, alter, update, reform, rectify, change
❌ keep, maintain, ignore, worsen, leave unchanged, preserve
📝 The committee voted to **amend** three clauses before signing the agreement.

**AMPLIFY** *(verb)* | বৃদ্ধি করা, বিস্তৃত করা, জোরালো করা
To make something louder, larger, or more detailed; to increase in strength.
✅ increase, enlarge, expand, boost, strengthen, extend, heighten, magnify
❌ reduce, diminish, lessen, minimize, shrink, quiet, suppress
📝 Social media can **amplify** both positive messages and harmful misinformation.

**ANARCHIST / ANARCHY** *(noun)* | অরাজকতাবাদী / অরাজকতা
(Anarchist) one who promotes the absence of government; (anarchy) disorder.
✅ chaos, disorder, lawlessness, rebellion, upheaval, revolution
❌ order, governance, law, authority, peace, control, stability
📝 Without leadership, the organisation quickly descended into complete **anarchy**.

**ANTAGONIST** *(noun)* | প্রতিপক্ষ, বিরোধী পক্ষ
A person who actively opposes or is hostile to someone; an adversary.
✅ opponent, enemy, rival, adversary, foe, competitor, nemesis
❌ ally, friend, supporter, companion, protagonist, helper
📝 The **antagonist** in the story was not evil but rather deeply misunderstood.

**APPEASE** *(verb)* | শান্ত করা, মিটিয়ে দেওয়া
To pacify or placate someone by giving them what they want; to soothe.
✅ pacify, placate, soothe, mollify, calm, satisfy, conciliate, relieve
❌ provoke, aggravate, antagonize, anger, inflame, irritate, upset
📝 She brought gifts to **appease** her angry friend after the misunderstanding.

**ARBITRARY** *(adjective)* | খেয়ালী, স্বেচ্ছাচারী, যুক্তিহীন
Based on random choice rather than reason or system; capricious.
✅ random, capricious, whimsical, inconsistent, illogical, unreasonable
❌ systematic, logical, rational, consistent, reasoned, principled, fair
📝 The rule seemed completely **arbitrary** and had no logical foundation whatsoever.

**ARCANE** *(adjective)* | রহস্যময়, গুপ্ত, অল্পজ্ঞাত
Known or understood by very few people; mysterious and secret; obscure.
✅ mysterious, secret, obscure, esoteric, cryptic, little-known, hidden
❌ common, well-known, obvious, popular, familiar, accessible, clear
📝 The professor specialised in **arcane** aspects of medieval law that few had studied.

**ARCHAIC** *(adjective)* | পুরাতন, প্রাচীন, চলনবাহির
Very old or old-fashioned; belonging to an earlier period; outdated.
✅ ancient, old-fashioned, outdated, obsolete, antique, antiquated, old
❌ modern, current, contemporary, new, fresh, up-to-date, relevant
📝 The **archaic** legal language in the contract confused even seasoned lawyers.

**ARDOR** *(noun)* | তীব্র উৎসাহ, আবেগ, উদ্দীপনা
Great enthusiasm or passion; intense feeling or devotion.
✅ passion, enthusiasm, zeal, fervor, devotion, intensity, eagerness
❌ apathy, indifference, coldness, disinterest, lethargy, dullness
📝 She tackled every assignment with genuine **ardor** and commitment.

**ARTICULATE** *(adjective/verb)* | স্পষ্টভাবে বলা, সুবক্তা
Able to express thoughts fluently and clearly; eloquent in speech.
✅ eloquent, fluent, clear, well-spoken, expressive, coherent, lucid
❌ inarticulate, tongue-tied, unclear, mumbling, confused, incoherent
📝 She was an **articulate** speaker who could explain even the most complex issues simply.

**ASCERTAIN** *(verb)* | নিশ্চিত করা, খোঁজ করা
To find out something for certain; to establish the truth of something.
✅ determine, discover, find out, verify, establish, confirm, learn
❌ overlook, guess, assume, ignore, neglect, miss, leave unknown
📝 Police worked around the clock to **ascertain** the cause of the accident.

**ASSIDUOUS** *(adjective)* | পরিশ্রমী, নিরলস, সতর্ক
Showing great care and perseverance in one's work; diligent and persistent.
✅ diligent, hardworking, persistent, careful, industrious, thorough, devoted
❌ lazy, careless, negligent, sloppy, half-hearted, idle, inattentive
📝 His **assiduous** revision paid off when he scored perfectly on the exam.

**ASSUAGE** *(verb)* | প্রশমিত করা, শান্ত করা, মেটানো
To make an unpleasant feeling less intense; to soothe or satisfy.
✅ ease, relieve, soothe, calm, pacify, alleviate, appease, lessen
❌ worsen, intensify, aggravate, inflame, provoke, heighten, increase
📝 The cup of tea did little to **assuage** her deep anxiety about the results.

**ATONE** *(verb)* | প্রায়শ্চিত্ত করা, ক্ষতিপূরণ দেওয়া
To make amends for a wrong or injury; to compensate for past wrongdoing.
✅ make amends, compensate, repent, pay, repair, rectify, repay
❌ ignore, repeat, worsen, evade, avoid, deny, refuse to compensate
📝 He spent years trying to **atone** for the mistakes he made in his youth.

**AUDACIOUS** *(adjective)* | সাহসী, দুঃসাহসী, নির্লজ্জ
Showing a willingness to take surprisingly bold risks; shamelessly bold.
✅ daring, bold, brave, fearless, reckless, brazen, impudent, gutsy
❌ cowardly, timid, cautious, fearful, hesitant, meek, restrained
📝 The **audacious** plan to cross the Atlantic solo impressed everyone who heard it.

**AUGMENT** *(verb)* | বৃদ্ধি করা, বাড়ানো
To make something greater by adding to it; to increase in size or value.
✅ increase, enlarge, expand, extend, enhance, boost, add to, supplement
❌ reduce, decrease, lessen, shrink, minimize, diminish, cut back
📝 The new funding will **augment** the hospital's existing medical resources.

**AVERT** *(verb)* | এড়ানো, বিপদ ঠেকানো
To prevent or avoid something undesirable from happening; to turn away.
✅ prevent, avoid, stop, ward off, forestall, deter, evade, stave off
❌ cause, bring about, allow, invite, permit, encourage, create
📝 Quick thinking by the pilot helped **avert** what could have been a disaster.

**AWE** *(noun)* | বিস্ময়, ভয়মিশ্রিত শ্রদ্ধা
A feeling of reverential respect mixed with wonder or fear.
✅ wonder, amazement, reverence, admiration, astonishment, respect, fear
❌ contempt, indifference, disrespect, boredom, disregard, mockery
📝 Standing at the edge of the Grand Canyon filled her with complete **awe**.

## 🔤 B (Additional)

**BAFFLE** *(verb)* | বিভ্রান্ত করা, হতবুদ্ধি করা
To confuse someone so much that they cannot understand; to perplex.
✅ confuse, perplex, puzzle, bewilder, mystify, stump, confound
❌ clarify, explain, enlighten, guide, help understand, simplify
📝 The strange results **baffled** even the most experienced researchers.

**BANAL** *(adjective)* | একঘেয়ে, পুরনো ধারার, অর্থহীন
So lacking in originality as to be boring; trite and overused.
✅ trite, clichéd, ordinary, dull, unoriginal, hackneyed, stale, predictable
❌ original, fresh, exciting, novel, creative, unique, innovative
📝 The speech was full of **banal** remarks that failed to inspire the crowd.

**BANE** *(noun)* | বিপদের কারণ, কষ্টের উৎস
A person or thing that causes great distress or annoyance; a poison.
✅ curse, plague, annoyance, nuisance, scourge, torment, affliction
❌ blessing, benefit, advantage, boon, help, joy, comfort
📝 Procrastination was the **bane** of his academic life throughout university.

**BASTION** *(noun)* | দুর্গ, রক্ষার কেন্দ্র, ঘাঁটি
A projecting part of a fortification; something seen as protecting a principle.
✅ stronghold, fortress, defender, bulwark, citadel, pillar, champion
❌ weakness, vulnerability, surrendering point, open ground
📝 The university was regarded as a **bastion** of free speech and open debate.

**BELITTLE** *(verb)* | ছোট করা, তুচ্ছ করা
To make someone or something seem less important; to dismiss as trivial.
✅ demean, disparage, dismiss, minimize, ridicule, deprecate, mock
❌ praise, celebrate, honor, uplift, acknowledge, magnify, credit
📝 A good manager never **belittles** an employee's ideas in front of the team.

**BESTOW** *(verb)* | দান করা, প্রদান করা
To confer or present an honor, right, or gift upon someone.
✅ give, grant, award, present, confer, donate, impart, provide
❌ take, withhold, deny, refuse, withdraw, remove, revoke
📝 The university chose to **bestow** an honorary degree upon the celebrated author.

**BLEAK** *(adjective)* | নিরানন্দ, বিষণ্ণ, অন্ধকার
Lacking warmth or kindness; offering little or no hope; cold and bare.
✅ gloomy, cheerless, hopeless, desolate, dismal, grim, empty, cold
❌ cheerful, warm, hopeful, lively, pleasant, promising, inviting
📝 The economic outlook looked increasingly **bleak** as unemployment continued to rise.

**BOAST** *(verb)* | গর্ব করা, বড়াই করা
To talk with excessive pride about one's achievements; to brag.
✅ brag, crow, show off, vaunt, flaunt, gloat, exaggerate, proclaim
❌ humble oneself, understate, conceal, modestly mention, deny
📝 He never needed to **boast** — his record spoke loudly for itself.

**BREVITY** *(noun)* | সংক্ষিপ্ততা, সংক্ষেপ
The quality of expressing something in very few words; conciseness.
✅ conciseness, shortness, terseness, succinctness, laconism
❌ verbosity, length, wordiness, long-windedness, prolixity
📝 The best speeches combine **brevity** with depth — short and deeply meaningful.

**BURLESQUE** *(noun/verb)* | তামাশা, ব্যঙ্গ অনুকরণ
A comic imitation of something; an absurd or comically exaggerated version.
✅ parody, satire, mockery, caricature, farce, spoof, send-up
❌ tribute, praise, sincere imitation, honor, reverence
📝 The play was a clever **burlesque** of political debates that got loud laughs.

## 🔤 C (Additional)

**CAPITULATE** *(verb)* | আত্মসমর্পণ করা, নতি স্বীকার করা
To cease to resist an opponent or an unwelcome demand; to surrender.
✅ surrender, yield, give in, submit, concede, acquiesce, back down
❌ resist, fight, hold out, persevere, defy, stand firm, persist
📝 After weeks of negotiation, the union finally **capitulated** to management's terms.

**CASTIGATE** *(verb)* | কঠোরভাবে সমালোচনা করা, শাস্তি দেওয়া
To reprimand someone severely; to criticize or punish harshly.
✅ criticize, reprimand, rebuke, punish, chastise, scold, censure
❌ praise, commend, reward, compliment, support, excuse, forgive
📝 The judge **castigated** the defendant for showing no remorse whatsoever.

**CLAMOR** *(noun/verb)* | গোলমাল করা, জোরে দাবি করা
A loud and confused noise; to make a loud and insistent demand.
✅ uproar, commotion, noise, racket, demand, shout, outcry, din
❌ silence, quiet, calm, whisper, peace, tranquility, hush
📝 A **clamor** of voices erupted in the hall when the announcement was made.

**COGENT** *(adjective)* | যুক্তিসংগত, শক্তিশালী, প্রভাবশালী
Clear, logical, and convincing; powerfully persuasive and well-reasoned.
✅ convincing, persuasive, logical, compelling, sound, clear, forceful
❌ weak, illogical, unconvincing, flawed, vague, incoherent, invalid
📝 She made a **cogent** case for reforming the outdated hiring policies.

**COMPLIANT** *(adjective)* | বাধ্য, মেনে নেওয়ার মানসিকতার
Inclined to agree with others or obey rules; submissive; ready to comply.
✅ obedient, submissive, accommodating, agreeable, cooperative, flexible
❌ resistant, defiant, disobedient, stubborn, difficult, rebellious
📝 A **compliant** patient who follows doctor's orders recovers much faster.

**CONDONE** *(verb)* | মাফ করা, উপেক্ষা করা
To accept or forgive behaviour that is considered morally wrong or offensive.
✅ forgive, overlook, excuse, tolerate, pardon, disregard, ignore
❌ condemn, punish, prohibit, reject, oppose, censure, penalise
📝 The management refused to **condone** bullying behaviour in any form.

**CONTRITE** *(adjective)* | অনুতপ্ত, দুঃখিত
Feeling or expressing remorse at the recognition of having done wrong.
✅ remorseful, repentant, sorry, penitent, apologetic, regretful, ashamed
❌ unrepentant, arrogant, shameless, unapologetic, defiant, indifferent
📝 The **contrite** student apologised sincerely and promised not to repeat the error.

**CONUNDRUM** *(noun)* | জটিল সমস্যা, ধাঁধা
A confusing and difficult problem or question; a riddle.
✅ puzzle, problem, enigma, mystery, dilemma, riddle, difficulty
❌ simple problem, clear solution, easy answer, straightforward question
📝 How to balance economic growth with environmental protection remains a **conundrum**.

**COPIOUS** *(adjective)* | প্রচুর, বিপুল পরিমাণে
Abundant in supply; present in large quantities; extensive and plentiful.
✅ abundant, plentiful, ample, lavish, profuse, extensive, bountiful
❌ scarce, meager, sparse, insufficient, limited, lacking, deficient
📝 She took **copious** notes during every lecture to ensure nothing was missed.

**CREDULOUS** *(adjective)* | বিশ্বাসপ্রবণ, সহজে বিশ্বাস করে
Having an excessive readiness to believe things; too easily convinced.
✅ naive, gullible, trusting, unsuspecting, over-trusting, innocent
❌ skeptical, cynical, suspicious, questioning, doubting, cautious
📝 The **credulous** investor was easily convinced by the fraudster's promises.

**CRYPTIC** *(adjective)* | রহস্যময়, দুর্বোধ্য, গুপ্ত অর্থের
Having a meaning that is mysterious or obscure; hard to understand.
✅ mysterious, obscure, enigmatic, puzzling, baffling, hidden, coded
❌ clear, obvious, plain, transparent, straightforward, explicit
📝 Her **cryptic** message left everyone guessing what she really meant.

**DAUNTLESS** *(adjective)* | নির্ভীক, অদম্য, সাহসী
Showing fearlessness and determination; not easily discouraged.
✅ fearless, brave, bold, intrepid, valiant, courageous, undaunted
❌ fearful, timid, cowardly, hesitant, scared, weak, intimidated
📝 The **dauntless** explorer ventured into the uncharted jungle alone.

**DEBILITATE** *(verb)* | দুর্বল করা, শক্তি নষ্ট করা
To make someone or something very weak; to sap the strength of.
✅ weaken, enfeeble, exhaust, drain, incapacitate, undermine, sap
❌ strengthen, invigorate, energize, empower, boost, revitalize
📝 The illness completely **debilitated** him for nearly two months.

**DECEIT / DECEPTIVE** *(noun/adjective)* | প্রতারণা / প্রতারণামূলক
The action of deceiving someone; (deceptive) giving a false impression.
✅ fraud, trickery, dishonesty, deception, cunning, misleading, falseness
❌ honesty, truthfulness, transparency, openness, sincerity, candour
📝 His **deceptive** appearance of innocence fooled investigators for weeks.

**DECISIVE** *(adjective)* | দৃঢ়, নিশ্চায়ক, সংকল্পবদ্ধ
Settling an issue; producing a definite result; having the ability to decide.
✅ conclusive, definitive, resolute, firm, determined, clear-cut, final
❌ indecisive, wavering, uncertain, hesitant, inconclusive, weak
📝 The general made a **decisive** move that ended the stalemate within hours.

**DECORUM** *(noun)* | শিষ্টাচার, শোভনীয় আচরণ
Behaviour in keeping with good taste and propriety; social dignity.
✅ propriety, dignity, etiquette, protocol, good manners, formality, grace
❌ indecorum, rudeness, impropriety, bad manners, vulgarity, chaos
📝 She conducted herself with perfect **decorum** throughout the difficult proceedings.

**DEFAME** *(verb)* | মানহানি করা, কুৎসা রটানো
To damage the good reputation of someone; to slander or libel.
✅ slander, libel, malign, smear, tarnish, discredit, vilify, dishonor
❌ praise, commend, defend, protect, honor, uphold reputation, vindicate
📝 He threatened to sue the newspaper for attempting to **defame** him.

**DEMURE** *(adjective)* | লাজুক, শান্ত, শালীন
Modest and reserved in manner or behaviour; shy and quiet.
✅ modest, shy, reserved, quiet, coy, retiring, gentle, meek
❌ bold, outgoing, immodest, brash, assertive, forward, shameless
📝 The **demure** young scientist spoke so softly that the room had to lean in.

**DEPICT** *(verb)* | চিত্রিত করা, বর্ণনা করা
To show or represent by a drawing, painting, or words; to portray.
✅ portray, illustrate, represent, describe, show, render, picture, paint
❌ misrepresent, conceal, hide, distort, ignore, omit
📝 The documentary **depicts** life in the slums without romanticising hardship.

**DEPLORE** *(verb)* | নিন্দা করা, আফসোস করা
To feel or express strong disapproval of; to regret deeply.
✅ condemn, criticize, regret, lament, object to, oppose, censure
❌ praise, approve, celebrate, support, welcome, endorse, condone
📝 The minister **deplored** the violence and called for immediate calm.

**DERIDE** *(verb)* | উপহাস করা, ঠাট্টা করা
To mock contemptuously; to treat something as laughable; to ridicule.
✅ mock, ridicule, scorn, taunt, jeer, laugh at, sneer at, scoff at
❌ praise, admire, respect, honor, compliment, applaud, celebrate
📝 She was **derided** by colleagues for suggesting such an unconventional solution.

**DESPONDENT** *(adjective)* | নিরাশ, হতাশাগ্রস্ত, মনোবলহীন
In low spirits from loss of hope or courage; feeling hopeless.
✅ dejected, hopeless, dispirited, depressed, downcast, discouraged
❌ hopeful, optimistic, cheerful, encouraged, confident, upbeat
📝 After the third rejection, he became genuinely **despondent** about his chances.

**DETRIMENTAL** *(adjective)* | ক্ষতিকর, অপকারী, বিপজ্জনক
Tending to cause harm; injurious and harmful to something.
✅ harmful, damaging, injurious, adverse, destructive, hurtful, negative
❌ beneficial, helpful, positive, constructive, advantageous, useful
📝 Prolonged exposure to stress is **detrimental** to both mental and physical health.

**DEXTEROUS** *(adjective)* | দক্ষ, চটপটে, নিপুণ
Mentally or physically skilled; having or showing skill with the hands.
✅ skillful, adept, nimble, agile, clever, capable, handy, proficient
❌ clumsy, awkward, inept, uncoordinated, fumbling, incompetent
📝 The **dexterous** chef prepared the dish in less than three minutes flat.

**DILEMMA** *(noun)* | দ্বিধা, উভয় সংকট
A situation in which a difficult choice has to be made between two or more options.
✅ quandary, predicament, problem, difficulty, bind, deadlock, conundrum
❌ easy choice, simple decision, clear path, straightforward option
📝 She faced a genuine **dilemma** — tell the truth or protect her friend.

**DISCORD** *(noun)* | বিবাদ, মতানৈক্য, বেসুর
Disagreement between people; lack of harmony; conflict and strife.
✅ conflict, disagreement, strife, friction, dispute, division, tension
❌ harmony, agreement, unity, peace, accord, concord, cooperation
📝 The policy change created **discord** among members of the committee.

**DISPARATE** *(adjective)* | ভিন্ন, বিভিন্ন, অসদৃশ
Essentially different in kind; not able to be compared; strikingly different.
✅ different, unlike, contrasting, diverse, varying, unequal, distinct
❌ similar, alike, uniform, comparable, homogeneous, consistent
📝 The group was made up of people with **disparate** backgrounds and viewpoints.

**DOMINEER** *(verb)* | কর্তৃত্ব করা, জুলুম করা
To assert one's will over others in an arrogant way; to bully.
✅ bully, tyrannize, control, oppress, intimidate, boss, rule harshly
❌ serve, cooperate, assist, submit, yield, defer, respect
📝 He had a tendency to **domineer** over younger colleagues who couldn't push back.

## 🔤 E (Additional)

**ECCENTRIC** *(adjective/noun)* | অদ্ভুত স্বভাবের, খামখেয়ালী মানুষ
A person who behaves unconventionally; unusual and odd in behaviour.
✅ unconventional, quirky, unusual, strange, odd, peculiar, bizarre, weird
❌ normal, conventional, ordinary, typical, usual, mainstream, standard
📝 The **eccentric** professor often arrived wearing mismatched shoes.

**ELOQUENT** *(adjective)* | বাকপটু, স্পষ্টভাষী, প্রভাবশালী
Fluent or persuasive in speaking or writing; clearly expressive.
✅ articulate, expressive, persuasive, fluent, well-spoken, moving, forceful
❌ inarticulate, fumbling, unclear, tongue-tied, inexpressive, mumbling
📝 Her **eloquent** speech on climate change moved many to tears.

**EMBELLISH** *(verb)* | অলংকৃত করা, বাড়িয়ে বলা
To make something more attractive by adding decorative details; to exaggerate.
✅ decorate, adorn, ornament, enhance, elaborate, exaggerate, beautify
❌ simplify, strip, reduce, minimise, plain state, understate
📝 He tended to **embellish** his stories with details that weren't entirely accurate.

**EMULATE** *(verb)* | অনুকরণ করা, অনুসরণ করা
To match or surpass a person by imitation; to try to equal or excel.
✅ imitate, copy, follow, match, rival, model after, aspire to, replicate
❌ ignore, dismiss, differ from, deviate, avoid copying
📝 Young athletes often try to **emulate** the techniques of champions they admire.

**ENCHANT** *(verb)* | মুগ্ধ করা, জাদু করা
To fill someone with great delight; to put under a spell; to captivate.
✅ delight, charm, captivate, fascinate, bewitch, entrance, mesmerise
❌ repel, bore, disgust, disenchant, alienate, horrify, repulse
📝 The children were completely **enchanted** by the magician's performance.

**ENDEAVOUR** *(verb/noun)* | চেষ্টা করা, প্রচেষ্টা
To try hard to do or achieve something; an earnest attempt.
✅ attempt, try, strive, effort, work toward, aim, seek, pursue
❌ give up, abandon, ignore, neglect, surrender, avoid
📝 Despite setbacks, she continued to **endeavour** to finish the project on time.

**ENIGMATIC** *(adjective)* | রহস্যময়, বোঝা কঠিন
Difficult to interpret or understand; mysterious and puzzling.
✅ mysterious, puzzling, cryptic, obscure, baffling, inexplicable
❌ clear, obvious, straightforward, transparent, understandable
📝 The artist left an **enigmatic** smile on the painting's subject that fascinated viewers.

**ERODE** *(verb)* | ক্ষয় করা, ধীরে ধীরে কমানো
To gradually destroy or wear away; to diminish by degrees.
✅ wear away, corrode, deteriorate, undermine, weaken, crumble, decay
❌ build up, strengthen, protect, reinforce, preserve, maintain
📝 Continuous criticism began to **erode** his self-confidence over time.

**ERUDITE** *(adjective)* | পণ্ডিত, জ্ঞানী, বিদ্বান
Having or showing great knowledge or learning; scholarly and learned.
✅ learned, scholarly, knowledgeable, academic, well-read, intellectual
❌ ignorant, uneducated, illiterate, unlearned, unknowledgeable
📝 The **erudite** professor could speak knowledgeably about almost any subject.

**ESTEEM** *(noun/verb)* | সম্মান করা, শ্রদ্ধা করা
Respect and admiration; to regard with respect or high regard.
✅ respect, admire, value, regard, honour, appreciate, revere
❌ despise, disrespect, scorn, undervalue, disregard, belittle
📝 She was held in high **esteem** by all who worked with her.

**EXACERBATE** *(verb)* | আরও খারাপ করা, তীব্র করা
To make a problem, bad situation, or negative feeling worse; to aggravate.
✅ worsen, aggravate, intensify, inflame, magnify, compound, increase
❌ improve, alleviate, soothe, reduce, calm, solve, ease
📝 Ignoring the issue only served to **exacerbate** the existing problems.

**EXEMPLARY** *(adjective)* | আদর্শ, অনুকরণীয়, দৃষ্টান্তমূলক
Serving as a desirable model; representing the best of its kind.
✅ ideal, perfect, model, outstanding, admirable, excellent, commendable
❌ poor, substandard, disgraceful, unacceptable, mediocre, bad
📝 The team's **exemplary** conduct throughout the crisis was widely recognised.

**EXHAUST** *(verb)* | নিঃশেষ করা, ক্লান্ত করা
To tire completely; to use up all of something; to drain.
✅ tire, drain, deplete, use up, wear out, empty, consume, fatigue
❌ energise, restore, replenish, refresh, fill, revive, revitalise
📝 The long negotiations **exhausted** everyone at the table by midnight.

**EXORBITANT** *(adjective)* | অতিরিক্ত, অত্যধিক
(Of price or amount) unreasonably large; far beyond what is normal.
✅ excessive, unreasonable, outrageous, extreme, steep, overpriced, inflated
❌ reasonable, fair, moderate, affordable, acceptable, proportionate
📝 The hotel charged **exorbitant** prices during the festival season.

**EXPEDITE** *(verb)* | ত্বরান্বিত করা, দ্রুত করা
To make something happen faster; to speed up the progress of something.
✅ accelerate, speed up, quicken, hasten, facilitate, advance, rush
❌ delay, slow, hinder, obstruct, impede, stall, postpone
📝 The hospital worked to **expedite** the discharge process to free up beds.

**EXPLOIT** *(verb/noun)* | কাজে লাগানো; শোষণ করা
To make full use of and derive benefit from; to use unfairly for one's own gain.
✅ use, utilise, take advantage of, abuse, manipulate, capitalise on
❌ ignore, protect, neglect, overlook, waste, leave unused
📝 Some companies **exploit** cheap labour in developing countries for higher profits.

## 🔤 F (Additional)

**FALTER** *(verb)* | টলমল করা, দ্বিধা করা
To lose strength or momentum; to hesitate; to move unsteadily.
✅ hesitate, waver, stumble, waver, weaken, tremble, lose confidence
❌ proceed confidently, persist, continue firmly, advance, strengthen
📝 Her voice began to **falter** as she described the most emotional part.

**FASTIDIOUS** *(adjective)* | বিচক্ষণ, খুঁতখুঁতে, সূক্ষ্ম রুচির
Very attentive to detail; difficult to please; having refined taste.
✅ meticulous, picky, choosy, fussy, precise, particular, exacting
❌ careless, indifferent, sloppy, easygoing, uncritical, casual
📝 The **fastidious** editor corrected every misplaced comma in the entire manuscript.

**FERMENT** *(noun/verb)* | গাঁজন, অস্থিরতা, উত্তেজনা
A state of agitation or unrest; to cause agitation and excitement.
✅ unrest, agitation, upheaval, turmoil, commotion, stir, excitement
❌ calm, peace, stability, order, tranquility, quiet
📝 Political **ferment** spread across the country following the contested election result.

**FERVOUR** *(noun)* | তীব্র উৎসাহ, আবেগ, উদ্দীপনা
Intense and passionate feeling; enthusiasm or zealous devotion.
✅ passion, enthusiasm, zeal, intensity, ardour, excitement, devotion
❌ apathy, indifference, coldness, disinterest, lethargy, dispassion
📝 She spoke with such **fervour** that the entire audience was moved by her words.

**FLIPPANT** *(adjective)* | হালকা ভাবের, অগুরুত্বপূর্ণ মনোভাবের
Not showing a serious or respectful attitude; treating serious issues lightly.
✅ frivolous, disrespectful, glib, irreverent, casual, superficial, dismissive
❌ serious, respectful, solemn, considerate, sincere, thoughtful
📝 His **flippant** response to a serious question irritated the entire room.

**FLOUNDER** *(verb)* | কষ্ট পাওয়া, সংগ্রাম করা, টলমল করা
To struggle or have difficulty managing in water or mud; to struggle.
✅ struggle, stumble, have difficulty, thrash, flounder, lose one's way
❌ thrive, succeed, sail through, manage well, progress smoothly
📝 The new employee was clearly **floundering** without proper guidance or training.

**FORBID** *(verb)* | নিষেধ করা, বারণ করা
To order someone not to do something; to refuse to allow.
✅ prohibit, ban, prevent, block, disallow, outlaw, restrict, deny
❌ allow, permit, approve, authorise, encourage, support, enable
📝 The school rules **forbid** the use of mobile phones during class hours.

**FORMIDABLE** *(adjective)* | ভয়ঙ্কর, শক্তিশালী, চ্যালেঞ্জিং
Inspiring fear or respect through being impressively large, powerful, or capable.
✅ powerful, impressive, daunting, intimidating, mighty, challenging, fearsome
❌ weak, harmless, manageable, insignificant, trivial, simple, ordinary
📝 They faced a **formidable** opponent in the semifinals who had never lost.

**FORTHRIGHT** *(adjective)* | সরাসরি, খোলামেলা, সৎ
Direct and outspoken; straightforward; honest without hesitation.
✅ direct, honest, candid, frank, blunt, outspoken, open, transparent
❌ deceptive, evasive, indirect, dishonest, subtle, guarded, secretive
📝 She gave a **forthright** answer that surprised everyone with its honesty.

**FRAGILE** *(adjective)* | ভঙ্গুর, দুর্বল, নাজুক
Easily broken or damaged; delicate and vulnerable; not sturdy.
✅ delicate, breakable, weak, frail, brittle, flimsy, vulnerable, thin
❌ strong, sturdy, durable, robust, resilient, unbreakable, tough
📝 The ancient vase was extremely **fragile** and had to be handled with great care.

✅ lazy, idle, sluggish, lethargic, slothful, inactive, listless, inert

**INFAMY** *(noun)* | কুখ্যাতি, বদনাম, কলঙ্ক

✅ notoriety, disgrace, dishonour, scandal, disrepute, shame, ill-repute

**INSIDIOUS** *(adjective)* | প্রচ্ছন্নভাবে ক্ষতিকর, ধীরে ধীরে ক্ষয়কারী

✅ subtle, stealthy, cunning, deceptive, treacherous, slow-acting, harmful
❌ obvious, open, harmless, transparent, clear, beneficial, honest

**INTEGRITY** *(noun)* | সততা, নৈতিক শক্তি, সম্পূর্ণতা

✅ honesty, honour, virtue, sincerity, uprightness, ethics, trustworthiness
❌ dishonesty, corruption, fraud, deception, immorality, duplicity

**INTRINSIC** *(adjective)* | সহজাত, অন্তর্নিহিত, মূলগত

✅ inherent, innate, natural, essential, built-in, fundamental, core
❌ extrinsic, external, acquired, added, outside, incidental

**INVINCIBLE** *(adjective)* | অজেয়, অপরাজেয়, অপ্রতিরোধ্য

✅ illogical, unreasonable, absurd, nonsensical, foolish, senseless
❌ rational, logical, reasonable, sensible, sound, justified, coherent

✅ persistent, stubborn, tenacious, determined, unrelenting, resolute

**POLEMIC** *(noun/adjective)* | বিতর্কমূলক, তর্কযুদ্ধ, বিতর্কিত আলোচনা

❌ agreement, praise, harmony, approval, endorsement, commendation
📝 The book was a brilliant **polemic** against modern political apathy.

**POMPOUS** *(adjective)* | দম্ভী, আড়ম্বরপূর্ণ, ফুলিয়ে বলা
Affectedly grand, solemn, or self-important; characterized by excessive pride.
✅ arrogant, pretentious, self-important, boastful, grandiose, haughty
❌ humble, modest, simple, unpretentious, down-to-earth, sincere
📝 The **pompous** speech was full of long words but said very little of substance.

**PRAGMATIC** *(adjective)* | বাস্তববাদী, প্রায়োগিক
Dealing with things sensibly and realistically; practically minded.
✅ practical, realistic, sensible, rational, matter-of-fact, logical
❌ idealistic, impractical, theoretical, unrealistic, dreamy, fanciful
📝 A **pragmatic** leader focuses on what works rather than what sounds good.

**PRECLUDE** *(verb)* | বাধা দেওয়া, পূর্বে রোধ করা
To prevent something from happening or someone from doing something.
✅ prevent, stop, rule out, exclude, hinder, block, eliminate
❌ allow, permit, enable, facilitate, encourage, make possible
📝 His injury did not **preclude** him from returning to competition next season.

**PRODIGAL** *(adjective)* | অপচয়ী, অমিতব্যয়ী, উড়নচণ্ডী
Spending money or resources freely and recklessly; wasteful; extravagant.
✅ wasteful, extravagant, squandering, spendthrift, profligate, lavish
❌ frugal, thrifty, economical, careful, prudent, conservative
📝 His **prodigal** spending habits left him bankrupt within three years.

**PRODIGIOUS** *(adjective)* | বিস্ময়কর, অসাধারণ পরিমাণের
Remarkably or impressively great in extent, size, or degree; enormous.
✅ remarkable, extraordinary, enormous, impressive, vast, astounding
❌ ordinary, average, unremarkable, small, insignificant, minimal
📝 She displayed a **prodigious** talent for mathematics from an early age.

**PROFUSE** *(adjective)* | প্রচুর, অঢেল, উদার পরিমাণে
Plentiful; abundant; produced or occurring in large amounts or number.
✅ abundant, plentiful, copious, lavish, generous, excessive, bountiful
❌ scarce, meager, limited, sparse, insufficient, restrained, moderate
📝 She offered **profuse** apologies for the delay in responding to their message.

**PROPAGATE** *(verb)* | ছড়িয়ে দেওয়া, প্রচার করা, বংশবিস্তার করা
To spread and promote an idea, theory, or knowledge widely.
✅ spread, promote, disseminate, broadcast, circulate, publicize, transmit
❌ suppress, withhold, contain, block, hide, restrict, silence
📝 The organisation worked to **propagate** awareness about mental health issues.

**PRUDENCE** *(noun)* | বিচক্ষণতা, সতর্কতা, দূরদর্শিতা
The quality of being prudent; caution and good judgment in practical matters.
✅ wisdom, caution, foresight, care, discretion, good judgment, shrewdness
❌ recklessness, imprudence, foolishness, carelessness, rashness, impulsiveness
📝 Financial **prudence** in youth leads to greater freedom and security in later life.

Complaining in a petulant or whining manner; habitually grumpy.
✅ complaining, whining, peevish, irritable, grumpy, fretful, discontented
❌ content, cheerful, satisfied, pleased, grateful, agreeable, easygoing
📝 The **querulous** patient complained about everything from the food to the lighting.

## 🔤 R (Additional)

**RAMPANT** *(adjective)* | অনিয়ন্ত্রিত, ব্যাপকভাবে ছড়িয়ে পড়া
Flourishing or spreading in an uncontrolled way; excessively widespread.
✅ widespread, unchecked, out of control, unrestrained, pervasive, epidemic
❌ controlled, limited, suppressed, restricted, contained, rare
📝 Corruption was **rampant** at every level of the administration.

**RECALCITRANT** *(adjective)* | জেদী, অবাধ্য, নিয়ন্ত্রণকারীকে অস্বীকারকারী
Having an obstinately uncooperative attitude toward authority; stubbornly disobedient.
✅ uncooperative, defiant, disobedient, stubborn, unruly, rebellious
❌ cooperative, obedient, compliant, docile, manageable, willing
📝 The **recalcitrant** student refused to participate despite the teacher's best efforts.

**RECLUSE** *(noun)* | একাকী মানুষ, বৈরাগী, সংসার ত্যাগী
A person who lives a solitary life and tends to avoid other people.
✅ hermit, solitary person, loner, introvert, ascetic, monk, isolate
❌ socialite, gregarious person, extrovert, outgoing person
📝 After his wife's death, he became a complete **recluse** and rarely left his house.

**REDRESS** *(verb/noun)* | প্রতিকার করা, সংশোধন করা
A remedy or compensation for a wrong or grievance; to remedy or set right.
✅ remedy, resolve, address, correct, fix, compensate, repair, amend
❌ ignore, overlook, deny, leave unsettled, worsen, neglect
📝 She sought legal **redress** after being wrongfully dismissed from her job.

**REFUTE** *(verb)* | খণ্ডন করা, ভুল প্রমাণ করা
To prove a statement or theory to be wrong or false; to disprove.
✅ disprove, contradict, deny, rebut, invalidate, counter, discredit
❌ confirm, support, prove, validate, agree with, endorse, accept
📝 The scientist was able to completely **refute** the claims made in the study.

**REPREHENSIBLE** *(adjective)* | নিন্দনীয়, ভর্ৎসনার যোগ্য
Deserving censure or condemnation; morally wrong and completely unacceptable.
✅ blameworthy, condemnable, shameful, disgraceful, deplorable, wrong
❌ praiseworthy, commendable, acceptable, good, admirable, honorable
📝 His treatment of subordinates was **reprehensible** and led to his resignation.

**REPUDIATE** *(verb)* | প্রত্যাখ্যান করা, অস্বীকার করা
To refuse to accept; to reject as untrue, unfounded, or unjust; to disown.
✅ reject, deny, disown, renounce, disclaim, disavow, contradict, abandon
❌ accept, acknowledge, affirm, support, embrace, recognise, claim
📝 The government **repudiated** the treaty signed by the previous administration.

**RESILIENT** *(adjective)* | স্থিতিস্থাপক, ঘুরে দাঁড়াতে সক্ষম
Able to withstand or recover quickly from difficult conditions; tough.
✅ tough, strong, adaptable, flexible, durable, resistant, bouncing back
❌ fragile, weak, brittle, delicate, unable to recover, vulnerable
📝 Communities that are **resilient** recover faster from natural disasters.

**REVERENT** *(adjective)* | শ্রদ্ধাশীল, ভক্তিপূর্ণ
Feeling or showing deep and solemn respect; worshipful and respectful.
✅ respectful, devout, worshipful, deferential, awed, pious, admiring
❌ disrespectful, irreverent, contemptuous, mocking, scornful, rude
📝 The crowd fell into a **reverent** silence as the ceremony began.

**RHETORIC** *(noun)* | বাকশিল্প, বাগাড়ম্বর, প্রভাবশালী ভাষা
The art of effective or persuasive speaking and writing; impressive language.
✅ oratory, eloquence, language, persuasion, expression, discourse
❌ plain speech, direct talk, simple language, bluntness, silence
📝 The speech was impressive **rhetoric** but offered no concrete solutions.

**RIGID** *(adjective)* | কঠোর, অনমনীয়, নিয়মনিষ্ঠ
Not able to bend or be forced out of shape; inflexible in behaviour or attitude.
✅ stiff, inflexible, firm, unyielding, strict, hard, immovable, fixed
❌ flexible, pliable, adaptable, supple, soft, yielding, loose, elastic
📝 The **rigid** interpretation of the rules allowed no room for common sense.

## 🔤 S (Additional)

**SANCTIMONIOUS** *(adjective)* | ভণ্ড ধার্মিক, উচ্চ নৈতিক ভান করা
Making a show of being morally superior to other people; self-righteous.
✅ self-righteous, pious, hypocritical, smug, preachy, holier-than-thou
❌ humble, sincere, genuine, authentic, modest, unpretentious
📝 His **sanctimonious** tone during the debate alienated even those who agreed with him.

**SCRUTINIZE** *(verb)* | সূক্ষ্মভাবে পরীক্ষা করা
To examine or inspect closely and thoroughly; to look at very carefully.
✅ examine, inspect, study, analyse, investigate, check, assess, probe
❌ ignore, overlook, glance, skip, dismiss, miss, neglect
📝 The auditors were asked to **scrutinize** every transaction from the past year.

**SEQUESTER** *(verb)* | আলাদা করা, বিচ্ছিন্ন করা
To isolate or hide away from others; to segregate; to confiscate.
✅ isolate, separate, confiscate, seclude, withdraw, remove, sequestrate
❌ include, release, integrate, free, join, combine, unite
📝 The jury was **sequestered** for the duration of the high-profile trial.

**SERVILE** *(adjective)* | দাসত্বপূর্ণ, অতিরিক্ত বাধ্য, হাঁটুগেড়ে
Having or showing an excessive eagerness to serve and please; overly submissive.
✅ submissive, obsequious, fawning, subservient, slavish, grovelling
❌ assertive, proud, independent, dominant, confident, dignified
📝 His **servile** attitude toward his boss made colleagues uncomfortable.

**SINECURE** *(noun)* | সহজ চাকরি যেখানে কাজ কম, ভেতো চাকরি
A position requiring little or no work but providing a salary; an easy job.
✅ easy job, cushy position, no-effort role, undemanding post
❌ demanding job, hard work, labour-intensive position
📝 The post was seen as a political **sinecure** given to loyal party members.

**SKEPTICAL** *(adjective)* | সংশয়ী, সন্দেহপ্রবণ
Not easily convinced; having doubts or reservations; inclined to question.
✅ doubtful, suspicious, questioning, unconvinced, cynical, mistrustful
❌ trusting, believing, confident, convinced, accepting, credulous
📝 Scientists remain **skeptical** of claims that have not been rigorously tested.

**SLUGGARD** *(noun)* | অলস মানুষ, কর্মবিমুখ ব্যক্তি
A person who is habitually lazy and inactive; someone who avoids work.
✅ idler, lazybones, loafer, good-for-nothing, sloth, dawdler
❌ worker, diligent person, hardworking individual, active person
📝 He was the office **sluggard** who could never be found when work was needed.

**SOBRIETY** *(noun)* | সংযম, মাতালতাহীনতা, গম্ভীরতা
The state of being sober; seriousness and calmness in manner or approach.
✅ abstinence, seriousness, gravity, restraint, composure, moderation
❌ drunkenness, intoxication, frivolity, excess, levity, immoderation
📝 She spoke with a **sobriety** that conveyed the full weight of the situation.

**SOVEREIGN** *(adjective/noun)* | সার্বভৌম, স্বাধীন, সর্বোচ্চ ক্ষমতাসম্পন্ন
A supreme ruler; possessing supreme or ultimate power; fully independent.
✅ supreme, independent, autonomous, ruling, dominant, governing
❌ subject, dependent, subordinate, controlled, governed, colonised
📝 A **sovereign** state has the right to make its own decisions without interference.

**SPECULATE** *(verb)* | অনুমান করা, ঝুঁকিতে বিনিয়োগ করা
To form a theory without firm evidence; to invest in risky ventures.
✅ guess, theorise, conjecture, ponder, suppose, wonder, predict
❌ know for certain, confirm, prove, establish, verify, determine
📝 Experts could only **speculate** about the long-term effects of the new policy.

**SPURIOUS** *(adjective)* | নকল, জাল, মিথ্যা
Not being what it purports to be; false or fake; based on false reasoning.
✅ fake, false, fraudulent, counterfeit, bogus, inauthentic, forged
❌ genuine, authentic, real, true, legitimate, original, valid
📝 The lawyer tore apart the **spurious** claims made by the opposing counsel.

**STEADFAST** *(adjective)* | অবিচল, দৃঢ়, নির্ভরযোগ্য
Resolutely or dutifully firm and unwavering; not changing one's stance.
✅ firm, determined, unwavering, resolute, loyal, faithful, constant
❌ wavering, unstable, fickle, disloyal, unreliable, inconsistent
📝 She remained **steadfast** in her belief even when everyone else gave up.

**STIGMA** *(noun)* | কলঙ্ক, অপবাদ, সামাজিক ধিক্কার
A mark of disgrace associated with a particular circumstance, quality, or person.
✅ disgrace, shame, dishonour, mark, stain, blot, stigmatization
❌ honour, praise, acceptance, approval, recognition, prestige
📝 There is still a **stigma** attached to seeking help for mental health issues.

**STOIC** *(adjective/noun)* | নির্বিকার, সহনশীল, আবেগহীন
Enduring pain and hardship without showing feelings or complaining; impassive.
✅ unemotional, calm, impassive, indifferent, unaffected, detached
❌ emotional, sensitive, expressive, complaining, passionate, reactive
📝 A **stoic** attitude helped him stay focused during the most difficult trials.

**SUBDUE** *(verb)* | দমন করা, শান্ত করা, কম করা
To overcome, quieten, or bring under control; to make less intense.
✅ suppress, conquer, overcome, calm, quell, tame, restrain, control
❌ incite, encourage, inflame, strengthen, provoke, amplify, release
📝 The fire was eventually **subdued** after a six-hour battle by firefighters.

**SUBLIME** *(adjective)* | উদাত্ত, শ্রেষ্ঠ, অপূর্ব সুন্দর
Of very great excellence or beauty; impressive enough to inspire awe.
✅ magnificent, glorious, majestic, superb, transcendent, supreme, exalted
❌ mediocre, ordinary, ugly, terrible, inferior, awful, poor
📝 The sunset over the mountains was nothing short of **sublime**.

**SUCCINCT** *(adjective)* | সংক্ষিপ্ত, সংক্ষেপে বলা হয়েছে এমন
Briefly and clearly expressed; covering the essentials without excess.
✅ brief, concise, terse, compact, short, pithy, condensed, to the point
❌ verbose, long-winded, wordy, rambling, expansive, drawn-out
📝 His **succinct** summary of the report saved everyone an hour of reading.

**SULKY** *(adjective)* | রুষ্ট, মুখ গোমড়া করে থাকা
Morose, bad-tempered, and resentfully silent; quietly resentful.
✅ morose, glum, sullen, resentful, pouty, petulant, disgruntled
❌ cheerful, upbeat, pleasant, outgoing, content, lively, happy
📝 He sat in a corner being **sulky** after failing to get his way in the meeting.

**SUPERCILIOUS** *(adjective)* | অহংকারী, অপরকে তুচ্ছ মনে করে এমন
Behaving as if one is superior to others; condescending and disdainful.
✅ arrogant, condescending, disdainful, haughty, contemptuous, pompous
❌ humble, polite, respectful, modest, considerate, down-to-earth
📝 Her **supercilious** attitude made it impossible for anyone to approach her.

**SURPASS** *(verb)* | ছাড়িয়ে যাওয়া, অতিক্রম করা
To be greater than; to exceed in excellence, achievement, or quality.
✅ exceed, outdo, eclipse, transcend, beat, excel, overshadow, top
❌ fall short of, fail to match, underperform, lose to, lag behind
📝 Her results **surpassed** all expectations set by the school board.

**SYCOPHANT** *(noun)* | চাটুকার, তোষামোদকারী
A person who acts obsequiously toward someone to gain an advantage; a flatterer.
✅ flatterer, bootlicker, yes-man, toady, fawner, apple-polisher
❌ critic, honest person, independent thinker, opponent, challenger
📝 The minister surrounded himself with **sycophants** who never challenged his ideas.

## 🔤 T (Additional)

To contaminate or pollute something; to damage a person's reputation.
✅ contaminate, pollute, spoil, corrupt, stain, blemish, sully, infect
❌ clean, purify, restore, clear, rehabilitate, protect, honour
📝 The scandal **tainted** the entire organisation's reputation for years.

**TANGIBLE** *(adjective)* | স্পর্শযোগ্য, বাস্তব, বস্তুগত
Perceptible by touch; clear and definite; real and able to be demonstrated.
✅ physical, real, concrete, actual, visible, touchable, definite
❌ intangible, abstract, theoretical, invisible, vague, conceptual
📝 After months of work, they finally had **tangible** results to present to the board.

**TEMPERANCE** *(noun)* | সংযম, পরিমিতি
Abstinence from alcohol or other excesses; moderation in all things.
✅ moderation, self-restraint, abstinence, self-control, frugality, sobriety
❌ excess, overindulgence, intemperance, gluttony, immoderation
📝 He practiced **temperance** in all areas of his life — food, drink, and speech.

**TENUOUS** *(adjective)* | দুর্বল, পাতলা, অনিশ্চিত
Very weak or slight; not firmly based; thin and insubstantial.
✅ weak, fragile, thin, flimsy, insubstantial, slight, unconvincing, shaky
❌ strong, solid, substantial, firm, convincing, robust, reliable
📝 The connection between the two events was **tenuous** at best.

**TERSE** *(adjective)* | সংক্ষিপ্ত, কম কথায় বলা, রুক্ষভাবে সংক্ষিপ্ত
Sparing in the use of words; abrupt; brief and rudely brief.
✅ brief, concise, curt, short, succinct, clipped, abrupt, brusque
❌ verbose, wordy, long-winded, elaborate, expansive, loquacious
📝 His **terse** reply made it clear he did not want to discuss the matter further.

**TORPID** *(adjective)* | নিস্তেজ, অলস, নিষ্ক্রিয়
Mentally or physically inactive; sluggish; in a dormant state.
✅ sluggish, inactive, lethargic, drowsy, idle, dull, slow, dormant
❌ active, lively, alert, energetic, dynamic, vigorous, awake
📝 The **torpid** atmosphere of the office made productivity nearly impossible.

**TRANSGRESS** *(verb)* | লঙ্ঘন করা, সীমা অতিক্রম করা
To go beyond the limits of what is morally, socially, or legally acceptable.
✅ violate, break, disobey, overstep, infringe, trespass, breach
❌ obey, follow, comply with, adhere to, respect, uphold
📝 Those who **transgress** the law must face the consequences of their actions.

**TREMULOUS** *(adjective)* | কাঁপতে থাকা, ভয়ে বা উত্তেজনায় কাঁপা
Shaking or quivering slightly; timid and hesitant; nervously fearful.
✅ shaking, trembling, quivering, unsteady, nervous, timid, hesitant
❌ steady, calm, confident, firm, still, bold, courageous
📝 Her **tremulous** voice betrayed her anxiety despite her confident words.

**TRITE** *(adjective)* | ক্লিশে, পুরনো ও একঘেয়ে
(Of a remark or idea) overused and consequently lacking impact; commonplace.
✅ cliché, overused, hackneyed, stale, unoriginal, banal, predictable
❌ original, fresh, creative, novel, innovative, meaningful, profound
📝 The speech was full of **trite** phrases that inspired absolutely no one.

## 🔤 U (Additional)

**UBIQUITOUS** *(adjective)* | সর্বত্রবিদ্যমান, সর্বব্যাপী
Present, appearing, or found everywhere; seemingly everywhere at once.
✅ omnipresent, everywhere, universal, pervasive, widespread, prevalent
❌ rare, absent, scarce, limited, localized, nowhere to be found

**UNDERMINE** *(verb)* | দুর্বল করা, ক্ষয় করা, গোপনে ক্ষতি করা
To lessen the effectiveness, power, or ability of, especially gradually.

❌ strengthen, support, bolster, reinforce, build up, help, protect

**UNKEMPT** *(adjective)* | অপরিচ্ছন্ন, এলোমেলো, পরিপাটিহীন
Not tidy; having an untidy or dishevelled appearance; uncombed.
✅ messy, dishevelled, untidy, scruffy, shabby, neglected, disordered
❌ neat, tidy, well-groomed, clean, polished, presentable, orderly
📝 He arrived looking **unkempt** and exhausted after the overnight bus journey.

**UNPRECEDENTED** *(adjective)* | অভূতপূর্ব, আগে কখনো হয়নি এমন
Never having happened or existed before; without previous occurrence.
✅ unparalleled, novel, unique, extraordinary, unheard-of, remarkable
❌ common, ordinary, familiar, typical, routine, frequent, normal
📝 The storm caused **unprecedented** damage to the region's infrastructure.

**USURP** *(verb)* | অন্যায়ভাবে দখল করা, হড়প করা
To take a position of power or importance illegally or by force.
✅ seize, take over, overthrow, steal, grab, commandeer, hijack
❌ surrender, relinquish, give back, yield, return, give up, vacate
📝 The general attempted to **usurp** the president's authority during the crisis.

## 🔤 V (Additional)

**VACUOUS** *(adjective)* | অর্থহীন, ফাঁকা, বুদ্ধিহীন
Having or showing a lack of thought or intelligence; empty and meaningless.
✅ empty, mindless, shallow, stupid, inane, hollow, pointless, vapid
❌ meaningful, intelligent, thoughtful, deep, substantial, smart
📝 The celebrity's **vacuous** interview contained nothing of substance at all.

**VALIDATE** *(verb)* | বৈধ করা, নিশ্চিত করা, সমর্থন করা
To demonstrate or support the truth or value of something; to confirm.
✅ confirm, verify, support, prove, substantiate, authenticate, endorse
❌ invalidate, disprove, deny, contradict, refute, reject, negate
📝 The second experiment **validated** the findings of the first research team.

**VEHEMENT** *(adjective)* | তীব্র, জোরালো, প্রচণ্ড
Showing strong feeling; forceful, passionate, and intense.
✅ intense, passionate, forceful, powerful, strong, fierce, emphatic
❌ mild, gentle, weak, calm, subdued, quiet, unenthusiastic
📝 She was **vehement** in her opposition to any compromise on the core principles.

**VERBOSE** *(adjective)* | বাচাল, অনেক বেশি কথা বলে
Using more words than necessary; long-winded; tediously wordy.
✅ wordy, long-winded, garrulous, talkative, rambling, prolix, loquacious
❌ concise, brief, succinct, terse, pithy, short, laconic
📝 His **verbose** report could have conveyed the same information in half the pages.

**VINDICATE** *(verb)* | নির্দোষ প্রমাণ করা, ন্যায়সংগত প্রমাণ করা
To clear someone of blame or suspicion; to show that someone is right.
✅ exonerate, clear, justify, defend, absolve, prove innocent, uphold
❌ condemn, blame, convict, accuse, discredit, indict, charge
📝 The new evidence fully **vindicated** the defendant who had been wrongly charged.

**VIVACIOUS** *(adjective)* | প্রাণচঞ্চল, হাসিখুশি, উজ্জ্বল
Attractively lively and animated; energetically spirited and cheerful.
✅ lively, animated, spirited, bubbly, energetic, vibrant, cheerful
❌ dull, quiet, reserved, lethargic, subdued, introverted, gloomy
📝 The **vivacious** presenter kept the audience engaged throughout the long event.

**VOLATILE** *(adjective)* | অস্থির, উদ্বায়ী, পরিবর্তনশীল
Liable to change rapidly and unpredictably, especially for the worse.
✅ unstable, unpredictable, explosive, changeable, erratic, turbulent
❌ stable, consistent, predictable, calm, steady, reliable, settled
📝 The **volatile** political situation made investors extremely nervous.

**VORACIOUS** *(adjective)* | অতিলোভী, অতিভোজী, অতি উৎসাহী
Having a very eager approach to an activity; excessively greedy or hungry.
✅ greedy, insatiable, hungry, eager, avid, ravenous, gluttonous
❌ moderate, restrained, satisfied, content, temperate, abstemious
📝 She was a **voracious** reader who could finish two books in a single weekend.

(Of a cruel or violent action) deliberate and unprovoked; undisciplined.
✅ deliberate, unprovoked, malicious, reckless, willful, unjustified
❌ justified, provoked, considered, deliberate for good reason, restrained

**WHIMSICAL** *(adjective)* | খামখেয়ালী, অদ্ভুত কল্পনাশীল

❌ serious, predictable, conventional, rational, grounded, sensible

**WIELD** *(verb)* | পরিচালনা করা, ব্যবহার করা
To hold and use (a weapon or tool); to have and use power or influence.
✅ use, handle, employ, exercise, exert, apply, control, brandish
❌ surrender, release, give up, relinquish, abandon, ignore
📝 She has learned to **wield** her authority wisely and without arrogance.

**WISTFUL** *(adjective)* | আকাঙ্ক্ষী, আকাঙ্ক্ষামিশ্রিত বিষাদ
Having or showing a feeling of vague or regretful longing; pensively sad.
✅ longing, yearning, melancholy, pensive, nostalgic, reflective, wishful
❌ cheerful, content, satisfied, happy, pleased, untroubled, carefree
📝 She gazed **wistfully** at old photographs of her childhood home.
