## 🔤 A (Final Batch)

**ABIDE** *(verb)* | সহ্য করা; মেনে চলা
To tolerate or accept something; to remain or continue; to follow a rule.
✅ tolerate, endure, bear, accept, follow, comply, stand, put up with
❌ reject, resist, defy, oppose, refuse, disobey, break
📝 She could not **abide** the sound of someone chewing loudly at the table.

**ABOUND** *(verb)* | প্রচুর থাকা, ভরপুর হওয়া
To exist in large numbers or amounts; to be plentiful; to teem with.
✅ teem, overflow, be plentiful, be full of, swarm, be rich in, flourish
❌ lack, be scarce, be deficient, be absent, be missing
📝 The rainforest **abounds** with species that scientists are yet to discover.

**ABRUPT** *(adjective)* | হঠাৎ, অপ্রত্যাশিত, রুক্ষ
Sudden and unexpected; steep; curt or rude in speech or manner.
✅ sudden, unexpected, hasty, curt, rude, sharp, steep, brusque
❌ gradual, gentle, expected, polite, smooth, courteous, leisurely
📝 His **abrupt** departure from the meeting left everyone confused and concerned.

**ABSOLUTION** *(noun)* | মুক্তি, পাপমোচন, ক্ষমা
Formal release from guilt, obligation, or punishment; formal forgiveness.
✅ forgiveness, pardon, exoneration, release, acquittal, clemency, discharge
❌ condemnation, blame, punishment, accusation, conviction, sentence
📝 He sought **absolution** for wrongs he had committed many years earlier.

**ACCEDE** *(verb)* | রাজি হওয়া, মত দেওয়া
To agree to a demand, request, or treaty; to assent or consent formally.
✅ agree, consent, comply, yield, assent, approve, concede, grant
❌ refuse, reject, deny, oppose, resist, decline, withhold
📝 After lengthy talks, the government finally chose to **accede** to the demands.

**ACRIMONY** *(noun)* | তিক্ততা, কটুতা, বিদ্বেষমূলক মনোভাব
Bitterness or ill feeling, especially in speech or manner; harsh hostility.
✅ bitterness, hostility, resentment, animosity, rancour, spite, harshness
❌ goodwill, warmth, friendliness, harmony, kindness, pleasantness
📝 The divorce proceedings were marked by intense **acrimony** between both parties.

**ACQUIT** *(verb)* | বেকসুর খালাস করা, মুক্তি দেওয়া
To formally declare that someone is not guilty of a criminal charge.
✅ exonerate, clear, absolve, free, vindicate, discharge, pardon, release
❌ convict, sentence, condemn, blame, imprison, charge, find guilty
📝 The jury voted to **acquit** the defendant after just two hours of deliberation.

**ADAMANTINE** *(adjective)* | হীরার মতো কঠিন, অনমনীয়
Unbreakable and impenetrable; extremely hard; too strong to be overcome.
✅ unyielding, unbreakable, impenetrable, rock-hard, indestructible, rigid
❌ soft, breakable, flexible, yielding, fragile, malleable, pliable
📝 Her **adamantine** resolve could not be shaken by any argument.

**ADAGE** *(noun)* | প্রবাদ বাক্য, পুরনো প্রজ্ঞার কথা
A short statement expressing a general truth or piece of advice; a proverb.
✅ proverb, saying, maxim, axiom, truism, motto, aphorism, wisdom
❌ falsehood, untrue statement, lie, misconception, error
📝 The **adage** "actions speak louder than words" has never been truer.

**ADULATION** *(noun)* | অতিরিক্ত প্রশংসা, তোষামোদ
Excessive admiration or praise; obsequious flattery or adoration.
✅ flattery, praise, excessive admiration, glorification, hero worship, fawning
❌ criticism, condemnation, insult, contempt, disapproval, censure
📝 The celebrity was surrounded by **adulation** wherever she went.

**AFFABLE** *(adjective)* | মিষ্টভাষী, সহজে মেলামেশার, আলাপী
Friendly, easy to talk to, and pleasant in manner; approachable.
✅ friendly, pleasant, approachable, easy-going, sociable, amiable, warm
❌ unfriendly, cold, distant, hostile, rude, unapproachable, difficult
📝 Despite his fame, he remained genuinely **affable** and easy to approach.

**AGGRANDIZE** *(verb)* | বড় করা, গুরুত্ব বাড়ানো, নিজেকে প্রসারিত করা
To increase the power, status, or wealth of; to make appear greater.
✅ inflate, exaggerate, elevate, magnify, enhance, glorify, boost
❌ minimize, reduce, downplay, belittle, diminish, lower, humble
📝 Politicians sometimes **aggrandize** their achievements to win votes.

**ALLEGIANCE** *(noun)* | আনুগত্য, বিশ্বস্ততা, স্বীকৃতি
Loyalty or commitment to a superior or group; faithful devotion.
✅ loyalty, faithfulness, devotion, fidelity, commitment, dedication
❌ disloyalty, betrayal, treachery, rebellion, defection, desertion
📝 Soldiers swear an oath of **allegiance** to their nation before serving.

**ALTRUISM** *(noun)* | পরোপকারিতা, নিঃস্বার্থতা
Unselfish concern for the wellbeing of others; selfless generosity.
✅ selflessness, generosity, charity, benevolence, humanitarianism
❌ selfishness, greed, self-interest, egoism, narcissism, avarice
📝 Her **altruism** led her to donate half her income to charity every year.

**AMBIENCE** *(noun)* | পরিবেশ, আবহাওয়া, মাহোল
The character and atmosphere of a place; the mood or feeling it creates.
✅ atmosphere, mood, environment, feeling, setting, surroundings, tone
❌ cold environment, sterile place, harsh surroundings, hostile setting
📝 The **ambience** of the candlelit restaurant was perfect for a romantic evening.

**AMICABLE** *(adjective)* | বন্ধুত্বপূর্ণ, মিলনশীল, শান্তিপূর্ণ
Having a spirit of friendliness; without serious disagreement or rancour.
✅ friendly, peaceable, harmonious, cooperative, pleasant, cordial, agreeable
❌ hostile, antagonistic, bitter, unfriendly, contentious, aggressive
📝 The two companies reached an **amicable** settlement without going to court.

**AMNESTY** *(noun)* | সাধারণ ক্ষমা, মাফ করা (সরকারিভাবে)
An official pardon for people convicted of political offences; a general pardon.
✅ pardon, forgiveness, clemency, reprieve, absolution, discharge
❌ punishment, condemnation, sentence, conviction, imprisonment
📝 The government declared an **amnesty** for all minor political offenders.

**ANECDOTE** *(noun)* | ছোট গল্প, ঘটনার বিবরণ
A short amusing or interesting story about a real incident or person.
✅ story, tale, account, narrative, episode, incident, reminiscence
❌ fiction, fabrication, lie, invention, falsehood
📝 He began his speech with a funny **anecdote** that put the audience at ease.

**ANIMOSITY** *(noun)* | শত্রুতা, বিদ্বেষ, বিরাগ
Strong hostility and active dislike; active hatred between people.
✅ hostility, hatred, enmity, antagonism, bitterness, resentment, acrimony
❌ friendship, goodwill, harmony, fondness, affection, love, amity
📝 Years of **animosity** between the neighbours finally erupted into a public dispute.

**ANNIHILATE** *(verb)* | ধ্বংস করা, সম্পূর্ণ নির্মূল করা
To destroy utterly; to obliterate completely; to wipe out all existence of.
✅ destroy, obliterate, eliminate, wipe out, eradicate, devastate, demolish
❌ create, build, preserve, protect, save, nurture, maintain
📝 The fire **annihilated** the entire building within a matter of hours.

**ANTAGONISM** *(noun)* | বিরোধিতা, শত্রুতা, প্রতিরোধ
Active hostility or opposition between people or groups; conflict.
✅ hostility, opposition, conflict, enmity, antipathy, rivalry, resistance
❌ cooperation, harmony, friendship, alliance, agreement, support
📝 The **antagonism** between the two departments was slowing down the whole project.

**ANTIQUATED** *(adjective)* | পুরাতন, অচল, কালবাহিত
Very old or old-fashioned; no longer relevant or appropriate; outdated.
✅ outdated, archaic, obsolete, old-fashioned, antique, bygone, ancient
❌ modern, current, up-to-date, contemporary, new, fresh, relevant
📝 The company's **antiquated** computer systems were long overdue for replacement.

**ANTIPATHY** *(noun)* | বিরাগ, ঘৃণা, অপছন্দ
A deep-seated feeling of dislike; strong aversion toward someone or something.
✅ dislike, hatred, aversion, hostility, repugnance, disgust, enmity
❌ liking, fondness, attraction, love, affection, admiration, sympathy
📝 She felt an immediate **antipathy** toward the new manager from their first meeting.

**APEX** *(noun)* | শীর্ষ, চূড়া, সর্বোচ্চ বিন্দু
The top or highest point of something; the peak of achievement.
✅ peak, pinnacle, summit, zenith, top, height, acme, crown
❌ base, bottom, nadir, lowest point, trough, floor, depth
📝 Winning the world championship was the **apex** of his sporting career.

**APOCRYPHAL** *(adjective)* | সন্দেহজনক, অবিশ্বাসযোগ্য, কিংবদন্তি ঘেঁষা
(Of a story or statement) of doubtful authenticity; probably not true.
✅ dubious, questionable, unverified, legendary, fabricated, fictitious
❌ authentic, verified, genuine, reliable, true, factual, historical
📝 The story of his swimming across the river in winter is almost certainly **apocryphal**.

**APPREHENSIVE** *(adjective)* | আশঙ্কিত, ভয়ে সচেতন, উদ্বিগ্ন
Anxious or fearful that something bad or unpleasant will happen; worried.
✅ anxious, worried, fearful, nervous, uneasy, concerned, tense, troubled
❌ confident, calm, reassured, fearless, relaxed, certain, composed
📝 She was **apprehensive** about the surgery but trusted her doctor completely.

**APT** *(adjective)* | উপযুক্ত, যথাযথ, প্রাসঙ্গিক
Appropriate or suitable in the circumstances; having a tendency; clever.
✅ suitable, appropriate, fitting, relevant, proper, pertinent, apt, spot-on
❌ inappropriate, unsuitable, irrelevant, improper, unfit, out of place

An attitude of superiority and contempt for others; excessive pride.

## 🔤 B (Final Batch)

✅ dull, insipid, tasteless, boring, uninteresting, flat, uninspiring
❌ stimulating, flavourful, exciting, interesting, lively, strong, bold

**BLASPHEMY** *(noun)* | ঈশ্বর বা ধর্মের নিন্দা

❌ reverence, piety, devotion, worship, respect, sacredness

**BLISS** *(noun)* | পরম আনন্দ, সুখ, পরম সুখ

❌ misery, sadness, despair, anguish, suffering, wretchedness

**BLITHE** *(adjective)* | উদাসীন, নিশ্চিন্ত, হালকা মনের

❌ concerned, serious, careful, worried, troubled, anxious, alert

✅ foretell, predict, indicate, portend, augur, presage, forecast
❌ contradict, assure, guarantee, deny, disprove

✅ dreadful, severe, terrible, desperate, urgent, serious, alarming
❌ mild, manageable, minor, unimportant, trivial, hopeful, positive
📝 The charity appealed for help amid **dire** warnings about famine in the region.

**DISCONCERT** *(verb)* | বিচলিত করা, অস্থির করা
To disturb the composure of; to unsettle; to make someone feel uneasy.
✅ unsettle, disturb, confuse, perturb, unnerve, bother, throw off
❌ reassure, calm, comfort, settle, steady, encourage, soothe
📝 The unexpected question **disconcerted** the spokesperson during the interview.

**DISCORDANT** *(adjective)* | বেসুর, অসামঞ্জস্যপূর্ণ, মতভেদপূর্ণ
Not in harmony; disagreeing; unpleasant to listen to.
✅ harsh, clashing, jarring, dissonant, inconsistent, conflicting, inharmonious
❌ harmonious, pleasant, agreeable, consistent, unified, concordant
📝 The **discordant** opinions within the team made progress nearly impossible.

**DISDAIN** *(noun/verb)* | অবজ্ঞা, তাচ্ছিল্য, ঘৃণামিশ্রিত অবজ্ঞা
The feeling that something is unworthy of consideration; contempt.
✅ contempt, scorn, disrespect, derision, arrogance, superciliousness
❌ respect, admiration, regard, appreciation, esteem, honour
📝 She looked at the mediocre work with barely concealed **disdain**.

**DISSENT** *(noun/verb)* | দ্বিমত, বিরোধিতা
The holding or expression of opinions at variance with those held officially.
✅ disagreement, opposition, objection, protest, defiance, conflict
❌ agreement, conformity, consent, accord, compliance, approval
📝 One member of the panel registered his **dissent** by leaving the room.

**DIVERGE** *(verb)* | বিভিন্নভাবে যাওয়া, আলাদা হওয়া
To develop in different directions; to depart from a set course or standard.
✅ separate, split, differ, branch off, deviate, vary, fork
❌ converge, unite, agree, meet, join, merge, come together
📝 Our opinions began to **diverge** as we went deeper into the discussion.

**DOLEFUL** *(adjective)* | দুঃখী, বিষণ্ণ, শোকাকুল
Feeling or expressing great sorrow or distress; mournful and sad.
✅ sorrowful, mournful, sad, woeful, gloomy, melancholy, wistful
❌ cheerful, joyful, happy, bright, lively, upbeat, contented
📝 She gave a **doleful** sigh before delivering the news no one wanted to hear.

**EMPATHY** *(noun)* | সহানুভূতি, অন্যের অনুভূতি বোঝার ক্ষমতা
The ability to understand and share the feelings of another person.
✅ compassion, understanding, sympathy, sensitivity, rapport, care
❌ apathy, indifference, callousness, coldness, insensitivity
📝 Great teachers show **empathy** for students who are struggling.

**EUPHORIA** *(noun)* | অত্যানন্দ, অসাধারণ আনন্দ
A feeling or state of intense excitement and happiness; elation.
✅ elation, joy, ecstasy, bliss, delight, rapture, happiness, exhilaration
❌ depression, sadness, misery, gloom, despair, anguish, suffering
📝 There was a sense of national **euphoria** after the team won the championship.

**EVOKE** *(verb)* | উদ্রেক করা, স্মরণ করা, জাগানো
To bring or recall a feeling, memory, or image to the conscious mind.
✅ invoke, summon, bring to mind, arouse, inspire, awaken, provoke
❌ suppress, forget, ignore, dismiss, bury, quell, extinguish
📝 The old photograph **evoked** memories of a childhood she had almost forgotten.

**EXALT** *(verb)* | উঁচুতে তোলা, গৌরবান্বিত করা
To hold someone or something in very high regard; to praise highly.
✅ praise, glorify, extol, elevate, magnify, honour, celebrate, laud
❌ demote, belittle, demean, condemn, criticize, lower, dismiss
📝 The nation chose to **exalt** its war heroes with a grand memorial ceremony.

**EXEMPLIFY** *(verb)* | উদাহরণ হওয়া, দৃষ্টান্ত দেওয়া
To be or give a typical example of something; to illustrate with examples.
✅ illustrate, demonstrate, typify, represent, show, personify, embody
❌ misrepresent, obscure, hide, distort, contradict, negate
📝 Her career choices perfectly **exemplify** the kind of courage this work demands.

**FEIGN** *(verb)* | ভান করা, ভণিতা করা
To pretend to be affected by a feeling, state, or injury; to fake.
✅ pretend, fake, simulate, act, bluff, put on, affect, imitate
❌ feel genuinely, be authentic, express truly, be sincere
📝 He **feigned** illness to avoid attending the meeting he dreaded.

**FORSAKE** *(verb)* | ত্যাগ করা, পরিত্যাগ করা
To abandon or leave someone or something; to give up or renounce.
✅ abandon, desert, leave, renounce, discard, give up, reject, quit
❌ keep, maintain, support, stay with, help, remain, protect
📝 He refused to **forsake** his principles even when it cost him everything.

**FRACAS** *(noun)* | হৈচৈ, মারামারি, ঝামেলা
A noisy, disruptive disturbance; a noisy quarrel or fight.
✅ brawl, uproar, fight, disturbance, scuffle, commotion, row, ruckus
❌ calm, peace, agreement, quiet, harmony, order, tranquility
📝 A **fracas** broke out near the stadium after the match ended controversially.

**FRUGALITY** *(noun)* | মিতব্যয়িতা, সাশ্রয়
The quality of being economical with money or food; avoidance of waste.
✅ thrift, economy, carefulness, prudence, sparingness, restraint
❌ extravagance, wastefulness, lavishness, profligacy, indulgence
📝 Years of **frugality** allowed her to retire comfortably before the age of fifty.

**GALLANTRY** *(noun)* | বীরত্ব, সাহসিকতা, ভদ্রতা
Courageous behaviour, especially in battle; polite attention shown to women.
✅ bravery, courage, valour, boldness, heroism, honour, chivalry
❌ cowardice, timidity, rudeness, dishonour, weakness, fear
📝 He was awarded a medal for his extraordinary **gallantry** during the rescue.

**GARRULITY** *(noun)* | বকবকানি, বাচালতা
The quality or tendency of talking too much; long-windedness.
✅ talkativeness, verbosity, loquacity, wordiness, long-windedness
❌ brevity, silence, conciseness, terseness, reticence, quietness
📝 His **garrulity** during meetings tried everyone's patience to the limit.

**GENIAL** *(adjective)* | মিলনশীল, উষ্ণ, বন্ধুত্বপূর্ণ
Friendly and cheerful; pleasantly warm and cheerful in manner.
✅ friendly, warm, affable, pleasant, agreeable, jovial, good-humoured
❌ cold, unfriendly, hostile, rude, unfriendly, sullen, grumpy
📝 The **genial** host made every single guest feel immediately at home.

**GESTICULATE** *(verb)* | ইশারা করা, হাত নাড়া
To use gestures, especially dramatic ones, instead of speaking or to emphasise.
✅ gesture, signal, wave, motion, point, indicate with hands
❌ stand still, remain composed, use words only, be still
📝 He **gesticulated** wildly trying to get the attention of the passing driver.

**GRACIOUS** *(adjective)* | মার্জিত, উদার, সদয়
Courteous, kind, and pleasant, especially in a condescending way from superiors.
✅ courteous, kind, polite, benevolent, warm, considerate, magnanimous
❌ rude, cold, unpleasant, inconsiderate, ungracious, mean, abrupt
📝 The queen made a **gracious** speech thanking everyone who had served her.

**GRANDEUR** *(noun)* | মহিমা, জাঁকজমক, গৌরব
Splendour and impressiveness; the quality of being grand, magnificent.
✅ magnificence, splendour, majesty, grandness, glory, impressiveness
❌ simplicity, humility, modesty, bareness, insignificance, smallness
📝 The **grandeur** of the ancient palace left visitors breathless with admiration.

**GRAPPLE** *(verb)* | সংগ্রাম করা, মোকাবেলা করা
To engage in a close fight or struggle without weapons; to deal with something difficult.
✅ struggle, wrestle, fight, deal with, tackle, contend, cope with
❌ ignore, avoid, give up, surrender, flee, sidestep, abandon
📝 For years, the city has had to **grapple** with the problem of homelessness.

**GRATUITOUS** *(adjective)* | অকারণ, অপ্রয়োজনীয়, বিনামূল্যে
Uncalled for; lacking good reason; unwarranted and unjustified.
✅ unnecessary, unjustified, unwarranted, unprovoked, baseless, pointless
❌ justified, warranted, necessary, reasonable, appropriate, called for
📝 The film was criticized for its **gratuitous** violence that served no narrative purpose.

**GRIEVOUS** *(adjective)* | তীব্র, ভয়ানক, অত্যন্ত কষ্টের
(Of something bad) very severe or serious; extremely painful; tragic.
✅ severe, serious, grave, dreadful, painful, terrible, awful, dire
❌ minor, mild, slight, insignificant, trivial, unimportant, harmless
📝 The nation mourned the **grievous** loss of so many young soldiers.

**GUISE** *(noun)* | বেশ, ছদ্মবেশ, রূপ
An external form or appearance; a style of dress; a false pretence.
✅ appearance, form, disguise, pretence, facade, semblance, cover
❌ truth, reality, genuine form, sincerity, authenticity, openness
📝 The spy operated under the **guise** of being a humble travelling merchant.

**HACKNEYED** *(adjective)* | মামুলি, ক্লিশে, পুরনো ধারার
Made unoriginal by overuse; lacking freshness because of overuse.
✅ overused, cliché, trite, stale, unoriginal, predictable, banal
❌ original, fresh, creative, novel, innovative, unique, interesting
📝 The essay was full of **hackneyed** phrases that added nothing to the argument.

**HALLMARK** *(noun)* | বৈশিষ্ট্য, গুণমানের চিহ্ন
A distinctive characteristic or feature; a mark of quality or excellence.
✅ feature, characteristic, trademark, sign, symbol, attribute, indicator
❌ flaw, defect, weakness, blemish, absence of quality
📝 Attention to detail is the **hallmark** of every product she creates.

**HAPLESS** *(adjective)* | দুর্ভাগ্যবান, বেচারা
Unfortunate and deserving of pity; unlucky; having no good fortune.
✅ unfortunate, unlucky, wretched, ill-fated, pitiable, luckless
❌ fortunate, lucky, blessed, successful, favoured, happy, thriving
📝 The **hapless** traveller arrived at the wrong airport for the second time.

**HARBINGER** *(noun)* | পূর্বাভাস দেওয়া মানুষ বা জিনিস
A person or thing that announces or signals the approach of something.
✅ omen, sign, herald, signal, indicator, portent, forerunner, precursor
❌ conclusion, result, aftermath, outcome, consequence, end
📝 The first robin in spring is often seen as a **harbinger** of warmer weather.

**HARMONIOUS** *(adjective)* | সুরেলা, সামঞ্জস্যপূর্ণ, মনোরম
Forming a pleasing or consistent whole; free from disagreement.
✅ melodious, pleasing, unified, peaceful, consistent, cooperative, agreeable
❌ discordant, jarring, conflicting, disjointed, chaotic, clashing, inharmonious
📝 The **harmonious** colours in the painting created a very calming effect.

**HASTEN** *(verb)* | তাড়াতাড়ি করা, ত্বরান্বিত করা
To be quick to do something; to move or travel hurriedly; to speed up.
✅ hurry, rush, accelerate, speed up, quicken, run, dash, advance
❌ slow, delay, dawdle, linger, wait, halt, impede, stall
📝 She **hastened** to correct the misunderstanding before it caused more damage.

**HEINOUS** *(adjective)* | জঘন্য, নিষ্ঠুর, অত্যন্ত খারাপ
Utterly odious or wicked; shockingly evil and beyond any justification.
✅ wicked, atrocious, monstrous, evil, abominable, vile, cruel, terrible
❌ good, admirable, kind, virtuous, honourable, decent, praiseworthy
📝 The court handed down the harshest sentence for such a **heinous** crime.

**HERESY** *(noun)* | ধর্মদ্রোহিতা, গোড়ামি বিরোধী মত
Belief or opinion contrary to orthodox religious doctrine; unorthodox thinking.
✅ blasphemy, unorthodoxy, dissent, apostasy, heterodoxy, nonconformism
❌ orthodoxy, conformity, traditional belief, piety, devoutness
📝 In those times, questioning the church's authority was considered **heresy**.

**HIERARCHY** *(noun)* | শ্রেণীবিভাগ, ক্রমসম্পর্ক, মর্যাদার ক্রম
A system in which things are ranked above each other according to status.
✅ ranking, order, chain of command, structure, pecking order, grading
❌ equality, flat structure, democracy, chaos, disorder, randomness
📝 She rose through the **hierarchy** of the organization over twenty years.

**HYPOCRITICAL** *(adjective)* | ভণ্ডামিমূলক, দুই মুখো
Behaving in a way that contradicts one's claimed beliefs; insincere.
✅ two-faced, insincere, sanctimonious, phony, deceptive, false, dishonest
❌ sincere, genuine, authentic, honest, consistent, truthful, transparent
📝 It was deeply **hypocritical** of him to preach honesty while cheating on his taxes.

**IDIOSYNCRATIC** *(adjective)* | ব্যক্তিগত, স্বতন্ত্র, অনন্য
Relating to idiosyncrasy; peculiar or individual; unique to a person.
✅ peculiar, distinctive, individual, unique, eccentric, characteristic
❌ common, typical, ordinary, normal, conventional, standard
📝 Her **idiosyncratic** teaching style baffled some but inspired many others.

**IGNITE** *(verb)* | জ্বালানো, উদ্দীপিত করা
To catch fire or cause something to catch fire; to arouse a strong feeling.
✅ light, set alight, kindle, spark, trigger, inspire, arouse, inflame
❌ extinguish, dampen, douse, cool, suppress, quench, discourage
📝 The protest march helped to **ignite** a national conversation about equality.

**ILLUMINATE** *(verb)* | আলোকিত করা, স্পষ্ট করা
To light up; to help to clarify or explain; to make intelligible.
✅ light up, brighten, clarify, explain, enlighten, reveal, shed light on
❌ darken, obscure, confuse, hide, mislead, cloud, complicate
📝 The documentary helped to **illuminate** aspects of the conflict that were little known.

**IMBUE** *(verb)* | ভরিয়ে দেওয়া, রং করা, প্রভাবিত করা
To inspire or permeate with a feeling or quality; to saturate or dye.
✅ fill, inspire, infuse, instil, permeate, suffuse, saturate
❌ drain, empty, deprive, remove, strip, cleanse
📝 Her training **imbued** her with a sense of duty that lasted a lifetime.

**IMPUDENT** *(adjective)* | বেয়াদব, ঔদ্ধত্যপূর্ণ, নির্লজ্জ
Not showing due respect for another person; impertinent and shameless.
✅ rude, cheeky, impertinent, brazen, bold, audacious, insolent
❌ respectful, polite, humble, deferential, courteous, well-mannered
📝 The child gave an **impudent** reply that shocked everyone in the room.

**INCANDESCENT** *(adjective)* | দীপ্তিমান, প্রচণ্ড উজ্জ্বল; অত্যন্ত রাগী
Emitting light as a result of being heated; filled with passion or rage.
✅ glowing, brilliant, luminous, radiant, blazing, furious, enraged
❌ dull, dim, extinguished, calm, composed, cool, collected
📝 He was **incandescent** with rage when he discovered what had happened.

**INCENTIVE** *(noun)* | প্রণোদনা, উৎসাহ দেওয়ার জিনিস
Something that motivates or encourages someone to do something.
✅ motivation, encouragement, stimulus, inducement, reward, impetus
❌ disincentive, deterrent, discouragement, punishment, obstacle
📝 A salary bonus was offered as an **incentive** for completing the project early.

**INDIGNANT** *(adjective)* | ক্রুদ্ধ, ক্ষুব্ধ, রাগান্বিত (অন্যায়ে)
Feeling or showing anger or annoyance at what is perceived as unfair.
✅ angry, outraged, offended, resentful, annoyed, furious, righteous
❌ pleased, satisfied, calm, indifferent, happy, approving, content
📝 She was deeply **indignant** at being accused of something she had not done.

**INDULGE** *(verb)* | প্রশ্রয় দেওয়া, নিজেকে মেনে নেওয়া
To allow oneself or someone else to have or do what they want freely.
✅ pamper, spoil, treat, gratify, satisfy, give in to, accommodate
❌ restrain, deny, discipline, withhold, refuse, deprive, limit
📝 She decided to **indulge** herself with a long holiday after the difficult year.

**INEPT** *(adjective)* | অদক্ষ, অনুপযুক্ত, অপারগ
Having or showing no skill; clumsy and lacking competence.
✅ incompetent, unskilled, clumsy, awkward, incapable, inefficient
❌ skilled, capable, competent, adept, proficient, expert
📝 The **inept** manager's poor decisions led the project to a complete failure.

**INFURIATE** *(verb)* | অত্যন্ত রাগান্বিত করা
To make extremely angry; to enrage; to fill with fury.
✅ enrage, anger, irritate, madden, incense, provoke, exasperate
❌ calm, please, soothe, delight, pacify, appease, satisfy
📝 His casual attitude to deadlines began to genuinely **infuriate** the entire team.

**INHIBIT** *(verb)* | বাধা দেওয়া, রোধ করা, দমন করা
To hinder, restrain, or prevent an action or process; to suppress.
✅ prevent, hinder, suppress, restrain, restrict, block, hold back
❌ encourage, enable, promote, facilitate, allow, support, release
📝 Fear of failure can **inhibit** people from taking the risks needed to grow.

**INSIGHTFUL** *(adjective)* | অন্তর্দৃষ্টিসম্পন্ন, প্রখর দৃষ্টির
Having or showing an accurate and deep understanding; perceptive.
✅ perceptive, astute, discerning, observant, shrewd, wise, intelligent
❌ dull, unobservant, oblivious, ignorant, shallow, obtuse, unaware
📝 Her **insightful** comments changed the direction of the entire discussion.

**INSOLENCE** *(noun)* | ঔদ্ধত্য, অসভ্যতা, বেয়াদবি
Rude and disrespectful behaviour; impudent speech or conduct.
✅ rudeness, impudence, disrespect, arrogance, cheek, insubordination
❌ respect, politeness, courtesy, deference, humility, decorum
📝 The student's **insolence** toward the teacher was met with immediate suspension.

**INTERVENE** *(verb)* | হস্তক্ষেপ করা, মধ্যস্থতা করা
To come between so as to prevent or alter an event; to mediate.
✅ interfere, mediate, step in, interrupt, intercede, get involved
❌ ignore, stay out, withdraw, avoid, stand aside, let happen
📝 The teacher had to **intervene** to stop the argument from escalating further.

**INTROSPECTIVE** *(adjective)* | আত্মবিশ্লেষণমূলক, অন্তর্মুখী
Characterized by or involving reflection on one's own thoughts and feelings.
✅ reflective, thoughtful, self-analytical, contemplative, inward-looking
❌ outgoing, extroverted, impulsive, thoughtless, unreflective
📝 She became more **introspective** after the accident, questioning her priorities.

**INVECTIVE** *(noun)* | গালিগালাজ, কটু সমালোচনা, তিরস্কার
Insulting, abusive, or highly critical language; verbal attack.
✅ abuse, criticism, denunciation, tirade, rebuke, condemnation, attack
❌ praise, compliment, flattery, commendation, encouragement
📝 His speech degenerated into **invective** when he ran out of actual arguments.

**IRONY** *(noun)* | বিদ্রূপ, পরিহাস, অদ্ভুত বৈপরীত্য
A state of affairs that seems deliberately contrary to what one expects.
✅ sarcasm, paradox, contradiction, twist, incongruity, wryness
❌ sincerity, directness, straightforwardness, openness, transparency
📝 There is a cruel **irony** in the fact that the fireman's own house burned down.

**IRREVERENT** *(adjective)* | ধর্ম বা কর্তৃত্ব অবজ্ঞাকারী, ঠাট্টা করে
Showing a lack of respect for people or things that are generally held as serious.
✅ disrespectful, flippant, cheeky, impertinent, irreligious, impious
❌ reverent, respectful, pious, solemn, deferential, sincere
📝 His **irreverent** humour was refreshing, but sometimes crossed a line.

**JARGON** *(noun)* | পেশাগত ভাষা, কঠিন শব্দের সমাহার
Special words or expressions used by a particular profession or group.
✅ terminology, technical language, lingo, vocabulary, specialized language
❌ plain language, layman's terms, simple speech, clear language
📝 The report was full of technical **jargon** that most readers couldn't understand.

**LACHRYMOSE** *(adjective)* | কাঁদুনে, অশ্রুসিক্ত, সহজে কাঁদে
Tearful or given to weeping; inducing tears; mournful and sad.
✅ tearful, weepy, sorrowful, mournful, sad, emotional, melancholy
❌ happy, cheerful, dry-eyed, stoic, composed, unmoved
📝 She became **lachrymose** watching old home videos of her children as babies.

**LAUD** *(verb)* | প্রশংসা করা, গুণকীর্তন করা
To praise someone or something highly; to extol publicly.
✅ praise, celebrate, extol, commend, honour, glorify, applaud, acclaim
❌ criticize, condemn, belittle, censure, dismiss, disparage, denounce
📝 Her charitable work was widely **lauded** in the local and national press alike.

**LETHAL** *(adjective)* | মারাত্মক, প্রাণঘাতী
Sufficient to cause death; extremely harmful or destructive.
✅ deadly, fatal, mortal, murderous, poisonous, destructive, killer
❌ harmless, safe, non-toxic, beneficial, gentle, benign, healing
📝 The chemical had **lethal** effects on anyone exposed to it without protection.

**LEVITY** *(noun)* | হালকা মনোভাব, হাসিখুশি তুচ্ছতা
The treatment of a serious matter without appropriate respect; lightness.
✅ lightness, frivolity, flippancy, cheerfulness, humour, light-heartedness
❌ seriousness, gravity, solemnity, earnestness, weightiness, formality
📝 His tendency toward **levity** made it hard to take the meeting seriously.

**LICENTIOUS** *(adjective)* | স্বেচ্ছাচারী, অশ্লীল, নৈতিকভাবে মুক্ত
Sexually immoral; promiscuous; not restrained by law or morality.
✅ immoral, promiscuous, lascivious, wanton, dissolute, depraved, lewd
❌ moral, virtuous, chaste, restrained, decent, principled, ethical
📝 The **licentious** behaviour at the party shocked many who attended.

**LITHE** *(adjective)* | নমনীয়, চটপটে, সুডৌল
(Of a person or their body) thin, supple, and graceful; flexible.
✅ supple, agile, flexible, graceful, nimble, slim, slender, pliant
❌ stiff, rigid, inflexible, clumsy, heavy, awkward, immovable
📝 The gymnast moved with a **lithe** grace that looked completely effortless.

**LOATHE** *(verb)* | ঘৃণা করা, অত্যন্ত অপছন্দ করা
To feel intense dislike or disgust for something; to detest greatly.
✅ detest, hate, despise, abhor, find repulsive, dislike intensely
❌ love, adore, like, admire, cherish, appreciate, enjoy
📝 She **loathed** everything about the commute — the crowd, the noise, the time.

**MAGNIFY** *(verb)* | বড় করা, বাড়িয়ে দেখানো
To make something appear larger than it is; to exaggerate greatly.
✅ enlarge, increase, amplify, exaggerate, boost, enhance, intensify
❌ minimize, reduce, understate, shrink, lessen, downplay, diminish
📝 The media tended to **magnify** every minor mistake into a full-blown scandal.

**MALODOROUS** *(adjective)* | দুর্গন্ধযুক্ত, বাজে গন্ধের
Having an extremely unpleasant smell; foul-smelling and stinking.
✅ smelly, stinking, putrid, fetid, foul-smelling, rotten, repulsive
❌ fragrant, sweet-smelling, pleasant, aromatic, fresh, perfumed
📝 The **malodorous** alley made it impossible to walk past without covering one's nose.

**MANDATORY** *(adjective)* | বাধ্যতামূলক, অপরিহার্য
Required by law or rules; compulsory; not optional or discretionary.
✅ compulsory, required, obligatory, essential, enforced, binding, necessary
❌ optional, voluntary, unnecessary, elective, non-mandatory, avoidable
📝 Attendance at the safety briefing is **mandatory** for all new employees.

**MENDACITY** *(noun)* | মিথ্যাচার, মিথ্যাবাদিতা
The tendency to be dishonest; habitual lying; untruthfulness.
✅ dishonesty, lying, deceit, untruthfulness, falseness, duplicity
❌ honesty, truthfulness, sincerity, candour, integrity, openness
📝 The politician's **mendacity** was finally exposed by an independent investigation.

**MOLLIFY** *(verb)* | শান্ত করা, রাগ কমানো, ঠান্ডা করা
To appease the anger or anxiety of someone; to soothe or calm down.
✅ appease, calm, soothe, pacify, placate, assuage, soften, ease
❌ aggravate, irritate, provoke, anger, inflame, upset, antagonize
📝 She tried to **mollify** the angry customer with a sincere apology and a refund.

**MONOLITHIC** *(adjective)* | একচেটিয়া, বিশাল ও অভেদ্য, একক সত্তার
Formed of a single large block; large, powerful, and unwilling to change.
✅ massive, uniform, imposing, rigid, unchanging, inflexible, enormous
❌ diverse, flexible, fragmented, varied, adaptive, small-scale
📝 The **monolithic** corporation dominated the market with little competition.

**MOTIVE** *(noun)* | উদ্দেশ্য, কারণ, অনুপ্রেরণা
A reason for doing something; a factor that causes someone to act.
✅ reason, purpose, incentive, goal, aim, cause, intention, drive
❌ deterrent, discouragement, result, effect, outcome, consequence
📝 The police were still trying to determine the suspect's **motive** for the crime.

**MUTABLE** *(adjective)* | পরিবর্তনশীল, অস্থির
Liable to change; not fixed; subject to variation or alteration.
✅ changeable, variable, alterable, flexible, unstable, volatile, inconsistent
❌ fixed, stable, immutable, constant, unchanging, permanent, rigid
📝 Fashion is highly **mutable** — what is popular today may be obsolete tomorrow.

**MYOPIC** *(adjective)* | দূরদৃষ্টিহীন, সংকীর্ণ দৃষ্টিভঙ্গির
Short-sighted; lacking imagination, foresight, or intellectual insight.
✅ short-sighted, narrow-minded, limited, unimaginative, blinkered
❌ far-sighted, visionary, broad-minded, far-reaching, insightful
📝 His **myopic** focus on short-term profits damaged the company's long-term future.

**NONCHALANCE** *(noun)* | উদাসীনতা, নিরুদ্বেগ, নিশ্চিন্তভাব
The state of being calm and relaxed to the point of apparent indifference.
✅ casualness, indifference, composure, ease, coolness, unconcern
❌ anxiety, worry, concern, excitement, stress, nervousness, agitation
📝 She accepted the award with a **nonchalance** that struck everyone as surprising.

**NOXIOUS** *(adjective)* | ক্ষতিকর, বিষাক্ত, অস্বাস্থ্যকর
Harmful, poisonous, or very unpleasant; causing damage to health.
✅ harmful, toxic, poisonous, dangerous, deadly, detrimental, unhealthy
❌ harmless, safe, beneficial, healthy, wholesome, non-toxic, good
📝 The factory was producing **noxious** fumes that endangered the surrounding area.

**NULLIFY** *(verb)* | বাতিল করা, অকার্যকর করা
To make legally null and void; to cancel out the effect of something.
✅ cancel, invalidate, void, annul, abolish, revoke, negate, countermand
❌ validate, uphold, confirm, enforce, support, maintain, ratify
📝 The court moved to **nullify** the election results in the contested constituency.

**OBSEQUIOUS** *(adjective)* | অত্যন্ত বাধ্য, তোষামোদকারী, চাটুকার
Obedient or attentive to an excessive degree; fawning and servile.
✅ sycophantic, fawning, flattering, servile, slavish, ingratiating
❌ assertive, confident, dignified, honest, independent, critical
📝 His **obsequious** behaviour toward the boss made his colleagues deeply uncomfortable.

**OBTUSE** *(adjective)* | বোকা, মন্দবুদ্ধি, সহজে বোঝে না
Annoyingly insensitive or slow to understand; mentally dull; not sharp.
✅ dull, slow, dim, thick, stupid, unintelligent, uncomprehending
❌ sharp, intelligent, clever, perceptive, quick, astute, smart
📝 He was too **obtuse** to realize that his comments were causing offence.

**ODIOUS** *(adjective)* | ঘৃণ্য, বিরক্তিকর, অসহ্য
Extremely unpleasant; repulsive; arousing or deserving hatred.
✅ hateful, detestable, repulsive, disgusting, loathsome, horrible
❌ pleasant, lovable, attractive, charming, delightful, admirable
📝 The **odious** behaviour of the bully was finally reported to the principal.

**OMNIPOTENT** *(adjective)* | সর্বশক্তিমান
Having unlimited power; able to do anything; all-powerful.
✅ all-powerful, almighty, absolute, supreme, invincible, dominant
❌ weak, powerless, limited, helpless, vulnerable, restricted
📝 No government is truly **omnipotent** — all power has limits and checks.

**OPPORTUNISTIC** *(adjective)* | সুযোগসন্ধানী, মতলববাজ
Taking advantage of opportunities as they arise, regardless of principle.
✅ self-serving, exploitative, calculating, mercenary, expedient
❌ principled, altruistic, selfless, honest, consistent, ethical
📝 The **opportunistic** company moved quickly to fill the gap left by its competitor.

**OSTRACIZE** *(verb)* | সমাজচ্যুত করা, বহিষ্কার করা
To exclude someone from a society or group; to deliberately shut out.
✅ exclude, shun, banish, ignore, isolate, expel, reject, shut out
❌ include, welcome, accept, embrace, invite, integrate, befriend
📝 After the scandal, colleagues began to quietly **ostracize** him in the workplace.

**OVERBEARING** *(adjective)* | অত্যন্ত কর্তৃত্বপরায়ণ, জুলুমকারী
Unpleasantly overpowering; domineering; treating others as inferior.
✅ domineering, bossy, dictatorial, arrogant, bullying, oppressive
❌ respectful, considerate, humble, cooperative, encouraging, fair
📝 Her **overbearing** management style drove three talented staff members to resign.

**PACIFIST** *(noun)* | শান্তিবাদী, যুদ্ধবিরোধী
A person who believes that war and violence are unjustifiable; a dove.
✅ peace-lover, dove, anti-war, conscientious objector, non-violent
❌ warmonger, hawk, aggressive, militant, violent, combative
📝 As a committed **pacifist**, he refused to take up arms under any circumstances.

**PALTRY** *(adjective)* | নগণ্য, তুচ্ছ, সামান্য
Very small or meager; petty and contemptible; ridiculously small in value.
✅ meager, tiny, trivial, insignificant, negligible, inadequate, poor
❌ generous, substantial, significant, large, considerable, impressive
📝 The worker was paid a **paltry** sum that barely covered her bus fare each day.

**PARIAH** *(noun)* | সমাজচ্যুত, ঘৃণিত ব্যক্তি
An outcast; a person rejected by society or a social group.
✅ outcast, exile, leper, untouchable, reject, social outcast
❌ idol, hero, celebrity, respected member, leader, beloved person
📝 After the exposure of his fraud, he became a complete **pariah** in business circles.

**PATRONAGE** *(noun)* | পৃষ্ঠপোষকতা, সমর্থন
Support given by a patron; regular business given to a store or service.
✅ support, backing, sponsorship, funding, business, custom, favour
❌ opposition, withdrawal, rejection, neglect, abandonment, discouragement
📝 The theatre depended on the **patronage** of a few loyal wealthy supporters.

**PAUCITY** *(noun)* | স্বল্পতা, অভাব, কমতি
The presence of something only in small or insufficient quantities.
✅ scarcity, shortage, lack, insufficiency, dearth, deficiency, rarity
❌ abundance, excess, plenty, surplus, sufficiency, wealth, bounty
📝 The **paucity** of evidence made it impossible to convict the suspect in court.

**PENCHANT** *(noun)* | ঝোঁক, প্রবণতা, বিশেষ পছন্দ
A strong or habitual liking for something; a natural inclination or preference.
✅ liking, preference, fondness, taste, inclination, propensity, tendency
❌ aversion, dislike, disinclination, repulsion, antipathy, reluctance
📝 She had a strong **penchant** for mystery novels and bought one every single week.

**PERMEATE** *(verb)* | ছড়িয়ে পড়া, ব্যাপ্ত হওয়া, ভেদ করা
To spread throughout something; to pervade or penetrate.
✅ spread, penetrate, pervade, saturate, diffuse, infuse, infiltrate
❌ stay out, keep away, block, contain, prevent from spreading
📝 The smell of fresh bread **permeated** every corner of the small bakery.

**PERPETUAL** *(adjective)* | চিরস্থায়ী, অবিরাম, শেষ না হওয়া
Never ending or changing; occurring repeatedly; so frequent as to seem continuous.
✅ eternal, endless, continuous, constant, permanent, everlasting, ceaseless
❌ temporary, brief, occasional, sporadic, limited, finite, ending
📝 She grew exhausted from the **perpetual** demands made on her time and energy.

**PERTURB** *(verb)* | বিচলিত করা, অস্থির করা, মন খারাপ করা
To make someone anxious or unsettled; to disturb greatly; to unsettle.
✅ disturb, unsettle, worry, upset, alarm, trouble, confuse, bother
❌ calm, reassure, soothe, comfort, settle, ease, relax
📝 The unexpected phone call late at night **perturbed** him for the rest of the evening.

**PESSIMISTIC** *(adjective)* | নৈরাশ্যবাদী, হতাশাপূর্ণ
Tending to see the worst aspect of things; expecting bad things to happen.
✅ negative, gloomy, hopeless, cynical, defeatist, downbeat, despairing
❌ optimistic, hopeful, positive, upbeat, confident, buoyant, bright
📝 His **pessimistic** outlook on the project made it hard for the team to stay motivated.

**PETTY** *(adjective)* | তুচ্ছ, ক্ষুদ্র মনের, অনুদার
Of little importance; unnecessarily concerned with trivial matters; small-minded.
✅ trivial, minor, small, mean, unimportant, narrow-minded, spiteful
❌ important, significant, generous, broad-minded, major, noble
📝 She refused to get involved in **petty** office politics and focused on her work.

**PHILANTHROPIST** *(noun)* | পরোপকারী, দানশীল ব্যক্তি
A person who seeks to promote the welfare of others through generous donation.
✅ benefactor, donor, humanitarian, altruist, charity worker, patron
❌ miser, selfish person, taker, greedy person, exploiter
📝 The billionaire **philanthropist** donated half his fortune to education projects.

**PHOBIA** *(noun)* | ভয়, ভীতি (অযৌক্তিক)
An extreme or irrational fear of something; an aversion to something.
✅ fear, aversion, dread, terror, irrational fear, anxiety disorder
❌ attraction, love, fondness, enthusiasm, desire, preference
📝 Her **phobia** of heights made it impossible for her to board the cable car.

**PIOUS** *(adjective)* | ধার্মিক, ভক্তিপূর্ণ, নিষ্ঠাবান
Devoutly religious; having or showing a dutiful spirit of reverence.
✅ devout, religious, godly, devout, dutiful, faithful, reverent
❌ irreligious, impious, sinful, godless, apathetic, undevout
📝 She was a deeply **pious** woman who attended services every single week.

**PITFALL** *(noun)* | ফাঁদ, বিপদ, অপ্রত্যাশিত অসুবিধা
A hidden or unsuspected danger or difficulty; a concealed trap.
✅ danger, hazard, trap, risk, difficulty, snare, obstacle, stumbling block
❌ advantage, benefit, opportunity, safety net, asset, ease
📝 The report outlined the major **pitfalls** of investing in emerging markets unprepared.

**PLACATE** *(verb)* | শান্ত করা, মনোতুষ্টি করা
To make someone less angry or hostile; to appease or pacify.
✅ appease, calm, pacify, soothe, mollify, conciliate, satisfy
❌ provoke, anger, antagonize, agitate, inflame, upset, worsen
📝 Management attempted to **placate** angry workers with an offer of a small bonus.

**PLATITUDE** *(noun)* | ক্লিশে কথা, সাধারণ ও অর্থহীন কথা
A remark or statement that has been used too often to be interesting or thoughtful.
✅ cliché, banality, commonplace, truism, bromide, empty phrase
❌ original thought, fresh insight, novel idea, innovation, wisdom
📝 His speech was full of tired **platitudes** that failed to inspire or convince anyone.

**PLUNDER** *(verb/noun)* | লুট করা, দখল করা
To steal goods from a place or person using violence, especially in war.
✅ loot, raid, ransack, rob, pillage, steal, strip, maraud, sack
❌ protect, preserve, return, safeguard, give back, donate
📝 The invaders proceeded to **plunder** the city's museums and historical archives.

**POMPOSITY** *(noun)* | দম্ভ, আড়ম্বর, ফাঁকা গর্ব
The quality of being pompous; self-important behaviour or language.
✅ arrogance, pretension, self-importance, grandeur, haughtiness, vanity
❌ humility, modesty, simplicity, sincerity, down-to-earth manner
📝 The journalist mocked the politician's **pomposity** in her column the next morning.

**PRECOCIOUS** *(adjective)* | অকালে বিকশিত, বয়সের আগে পরিপক্ব
Having developed certain abilities or inclinations at an earlier age than usual.
✅ advanced, gifted, mature for one's age, early-developing, talented
❌ slow, delayed, late-developing, backward, behind, underdeveloped
📝 The **precocious** child was reading chapter books before starting school.

**PREDATORY** *(adjective)* | শিকারী, শোষণকারী
Seeking to exploit or oppress others; relating to natural predators.
✅ exploitative, aggressive, opportunistic, greedy, parasitic, wolf-like
❌ protective, benevolent, fair, generous, helpful, nurturing
📝 The law was designed to protect citizens from **predatory** lending practices.

**PREEMINENT** *(adjective)* | সর্বশ্রেষ্ঠ, অপ্রতিদ্বন্দ্বী
Surpassing all others; very distinguished in some quality; outstanding.
✅ outstanding, leading, foremost, dominant, supreme, unrivalled, chief
❌ ordinary, mediocre, unknown, insignificant, inferior, secondary
📝 She was the **preeminent** authority on ancient Egyptian architecture of her era.

**PREJUDICE** *(noun)* | পক্ষপাত, কুসংস্কার, পূর্বধারণা
Preconceived opinion not based on reason or actual experience; bias.
✅ bias, discrimination, unfairness, bigotry, racism, sexism, partiality
❌ fairness, open-mindedness, tolerance, equality, acceptance, justice
📝 Hiring decisions based on **prejudice** rather than merit are always harmful.

**PRESUMPTUOUS** *(adjective)* | অহংকারী, সীমা লঙ্ঘন করা, বেআদব
Failing to observe the limits of what is permitted; arrogantly taking liberties.
✅ bold, arrogant, overconfident, audacious, forward, impertinent, cheeky
❌ modest, respectful, humble, careful, restrained, considerate
📝 It was **presumptuous** of him to assume he was invited without being asked.

**PRODIGY** *(noun)* | বিস্ময়বালক, অসাধারণ প্রতিভাবান ব্যক্তি
A person, especially a young one, endowed with exceptional qualities or abilities.
✅ genius, talent, wonder child, gifted person, marvel, natural talent
❌ dunce, failure, average person, underachiever, mediocrity
📝 The young musician was heralded as a **prodigy** at just eight years old.

**PROFICIENT** *(adjective)* | দক্ষ, পারদর্শী, অভিজ্ঞ
Competent or skilled in doing or using something; highly capable.
✅ skilled, capable, adept, expert, accomplished, talented, competent
❌ inept, incompetent, unskilled, amateur, clumsy, inadequate
📝 She became fully **proficient** in three languages by the time she graduated.

**PROLIFIC** *(adjective)* | প্রচুর উৎপাদনকারী, বহুপ্রজ
Producing many works, results, or offspring; highly productive.
✅ productive, fruitful, fertile, abundant, generative, creative, busy
❌ unproductive, sterile, barren, sparse, inactive, limited, few
📝 The **prolific** author published an average of three novels every single year.

**PROPITIOUS** *(adjective)* | শুভ, অনুকূল, সুবিধাজনক
Giving or indicating a good chance of success; favourable; auspicious.
✅ favourable, auspicious, encouraging, opportune, beneficial, promising
❌ unfavourable, inauspicious, ominous, bad, threatening, discouraging
📝 The clear sky on launch day seemed **propitious** for the rocket's maiden voyage.

**PROTEST** *(verb/noun)* | প্রতিবাদ করা, আপত্তি জানানো
An expression of strong disagreement; to express disapproval or objection.
✅ object, oppose, complain, disagree, resist, demonstrate, challenge
❌ agree, support, accept, endorse, approve, comply, welcome
📝 Hundreds gathered to **protest** against the proposed closure of the hospital.

**PRUDISH** *(adjective)* | অতি নম্র, অতি সংযমী, ছুঁৎমার্গী
Having or revealing a tendency to be easily shocked by sexual matters.
✅ prim, proper, strait-laced, puritanical, conservative, moralistic, fussy
❌ liberal, open-minded, uninhibited, relaxed, broad-minded, permissive
📝 Her **prudish** reaction to the comedy sketch surprised those who knew her well.

**PUGNACIOUS** *(adjective)* | লড়াকু, ঝগড়াটে, যুদ্ধপ্রিয়
Eager or quick to argue, quarrel, or fight; aggressively belligerent.
✅ aggressive, belligerent, combative, quarrelsome, hostile, argumentative
❌ peaceful, gentle, cooperative, mild, pacifist, accommodating, calm
📝 His **pugnacious** negotiating style often got results but made enemies too.

**PUNGENT** *(adjective)* | তীক্ষ্ণ গন্ধের, ঝাঁজালো, কটু
Having a sharply strong taste or smell; having a sharp and biting quality.
✅ sharp, strong, biting, acrid, piercing, strong-smelling, caustic
❌ mild, bland, faint, subtle, pleasant, gentle, soft, weak
📝 The **pungent** aroma of garlic filled the entire kitchen and hallway beyond.

**QUIBBLE** *(verb)* | তুচ্ছ বিষয়ে তর্ক করা, খুঁটিনাটি নিয়ে ঝামেলা করা
To argue or raise objections about a trivial matter; to nitpick.
✅ nitpick, object, argue over petty things, cavil, split hairs
❌ accept, agree, be satisfied with, approve, overlook minor issues
📝 There is no point **quibbling** over a single word when the overall message is clear.

**RAMPAGE** *(verb/noun)* | হিংসাত্মকভাবে ছোটাছুটি করা, তাণ্ডব করা
A period of violent and uncontrollable behaviour; to rush about wildly.
✅ riot, go berserk, run amok, rage, devastate, storm, destroy
❌ remain calm, stay still, behave, control oneself, settle
📝 The escaped animal went on a **rampage** through the town before being contained.

**RAPACIOUS** *(adjective)* | লোভী, লালায়িত, যা পাওয়া যায় সব নিয়ে নেয়
Aggressively greedy or grasping; having or showing a craving for gain.
✅ greedy, avaricious, grasping, predatory, voracious, selfish, covetous
❌ generous, giving, selfless, content, satisfied, charitable, altruistic
📝 The **rapacious** developer bought up farmland for a fraction of its worth.

**RECANT** *(verb)* | পূর্বের মত প্রত্যাহার করা
To say that one no longer holds an opinion or belief; to formally withdraw.
✅ retract, withdraw, take back, renounce, disavow, revoke, disclaim
❌ affirm, maintain, uphold, confirm, stand by, assert, persist in
📝 Under enormous pressure, he finally chose to **recant** his controversial statements.

**REPLETE** *(adjective)* | পরিপূর্ণ, পেট ভরা, সম্পূর্ণ ভরা
Filled or well-supplied with something; completely full; gorged.
✅ full, filled, stuffed, crammed, brimming, loaded, abundant, sated
❌ empty, lacking, deficient, hungry, incomplete, sparse, insufficient
📝 The guidebook was **replete** with useful advice for first-time visitors.

**REPROVE** *(verb)* | তিরস্কার করা, সমালোচনা করা
To rebuke or censure someone; to express disapproval formally.
✅ rebuke, reprimand, criticize, censure, scold, admonish, chastise
❌ praise, commend, encourage, compliment, reward, approve, support
📝 The coach gently **reproved** the young player without embarrassing her in public.

**RESIGNATION** *(noun)* | পদত্যাগ; নিয়তি মেনে নেওয়া
The acceptance of something undesirable but inevitable; giving up a position.
✅ acceptance, submission, acquiescence, departure, yielding, stoicism
❌ resistance, defiance, ambition, refusal, perseverance, fighting spirit
📝 She accepted the outcome with quiet **resignation**, choosing not to appeal.

**RIGOUR** *(noun)* | কঠোরতা, কড়াকড়ি, কঠিন মান
The quality of being extremely thorough and careful; harsh and demanding.
✅ strictness, thoroughness, precision, exactness, harshness, severity
❌ leniency, carelessness, sloppiness, flexibility, looseness, ease
📝 The **rigour** of the academic programme prepared students well for professional life.

**RUEFUL** *(adjective)* | অনুতপ্ত, দুঃখিত, অনুশোচনাপূর্ণ
Expressing sorrow or regret, especially in a wry way; mournfully sorry.
✅ regretful, sorry, remorseful, apologetic, mournful, woeful, contrite
❌ unrepentant, shameless, proud, content, satisfied, unconcerned
📝 She gave a **rueful** smile when asked about the biggest mistake she had ever made.

**SCEPTICAL** *(adjective)* | সংশয়ী, অবিশ্বাসী, সন্দেহপ্রবণ
Not easily convinced; having doubts or reservations about something.
✅ doubtful, suspicious, questioning, unconvinced, cynical, distrustful
❌ trusting, believing, credulous, convinced, accepting, optimistic
📝 She remained **sceptical** about his promises until she saw concrete evidence.

❌ respect, admiration, honour, regard, approval, appreciation, esteem

**WOEFUL** *(adjective)* | দুঃখজনক, করুণ, শোকপূর্ণ

✅ sad, unfortunate, dreadful, pitiful, awful, terrible, deplorable

# 📚 EXTRA BATCH — High-Frequency CUET Words Not Yet Covered
