## 📚 BATCH — 107 New Words from Hindi Transcript Session 2

**ABDICATE** *(verb)* | পদ ত্যাগ করা, সিংহাসন ছেড়ে দেওয়া
To renounce or give up a position of power or responsibility; to step down.
✅ resign, relinquish, renounce, step down, abandon, quit, surrender
❌ retain, assume, claim, seize, hold, maintain, keep
📝 The king chose to **abdicate** the throne rather than face a civil war.

**ADJACENT** *(adjective)* | পাশে, সংলগ্ন, নিকটবর্তী
Next to or adjoining something else; close or nearby.
✅ neighboring, adjoining, bordering, contiguous, close, nearby, proximate
❌ distant, remote, far, separated, detached, apart
📝 The library is **adjacent** to the main hall, making it easy to access.

**ADVANTAGEOUS** *(adjective)* | সুবিধাজনক, লাভজনক
Involving or creating favorable circumstances; beneficial.
✅ beneficial, favorable, helpful, profitable, useful, expedient, gainful
❌ unfavorable, harmful, detrimental, disadvantageous, useless
📝 Starting early is **advantageous** when preparing for competitive exams.

**ALTRUISTIC** *(adjective)* | পরোপকারী, নিঃস্বার্থ
Showing a selfless concern for the well-being of others.
✅ selfless, generous, charitable, philanthropic, benevolent, unselfish
❌ selfish, self-centered, greedy, egotistic, mean
📝 Her **altruistic** nature led her to donate all her savings to the orphanage.

**AMENABLE** *(adjective)* | রাজি, সম্মত, সহযোগী
Willing to accept or act on suggestions; responsive and compliant.
✅ responsive, cooperative, willing, compliant, agreeable, open, receptive
❌ uncooperative, stubborn, resistant, defiant, unwilling
📝 He was **amenable** to changing the plan after hearing the team's feedback.

**ANOMALY** *(noun)* | অনিয়ম, ব্যতিক্রম, অস্বাভাবিকতা
Something that deviates from what is standard, normal, or expected.
✅ irregularity, oddity, aberration, deviation, exception, abnormality
❌ norm, standard, regularity, conformity, pattern, rule
📝 Scientists studied the **anomaly** in the data to find the root cause.

**ANTICIPATE** *(verb)* | প্রত্যাশা করা, আগে থেকে ভাবা
To regard as probable and prepare for; to expect or predict something.
✅ expect, foresee, forecast, predict, envision, project, look forward to
❌ doubt, ignore, overlook, miss, disregard, be surprised by
📝 A good leader learns to **anticipate** problems before they arise.

**ARBOREAL** *(adjective)* | গাছে বসবাসকারী, বৃক্ষসংক্রান্ত
Relating to or resembling a tree; living in or among trees.
✅ tree-dwelling, woodland, sylvan, forested, dendritic
❌ terrestrial, ground-dwelling, aquatic, subterranean
📝 Monkeys are **arboreal** creatures that spend most of their lives in trees.

**ASININE** *(adjective)* | বোকামিপূর্ণ, নির্বোধ, মূর্খ
Extremely stupid or foolish; utterly senseless.
✅ stupid, foolish, idiotic, moronic, absurd, senseless, brainless
❌ intelligent, sensible, wise, astute, clever, rational
📝 It was an **asinine** decision to ignore the safety warnings.

**ASSENT** *(noun/verb)* | সম্মতি, রাজি হওয়া
Agreement or approval, especially official agreement; to express agreement.
✅ agreement, approval, consent, concurrence, acceptance, accord
❌ dissent, refusal, disagreement, objection, denial, rejection
📝 The committee gave its **assent** to the new budget proposal.

**ASSERT** *(verb)* | দাবি করা, জোর দিয়ে বলা
To state a fact or belief confidently and forcefully; to declare.
✅ declare, affirm, claim, state, maintain, insist, contend, proclaim
❌ deny, retract, doubt, question, refute, negate, withdraw
📝 She **asserted** her right to speak at the meeting without interruption.

**BALEFUL** *(adjective)* | ক্ষতিকর, মারাত্মক, হুমকিপূর্ণ
Threatening harm; menacing; having a harmful or destructive effect.
✅ menacing, threatening, harmful, sinister, malevolent, ominous, deadly
❌ benign, harmless, benevolent, friendly, favorable, safe
📝 The wolf gave the traveler a **baleful** stare from across the forest path.

**BEWILDER** *(verb)* | হতবুদ্ধি করা, ধাঁধায় ফেলা
To cause someone to become perplexed and confused; to puzzle greatly.
✅ confuse, perplex, puzzle, baffle, mystify, disorient, flummox
❌ clarify, enlighten, explain, guide, orient, illuminate
📝 The contradictory instructions **bewildered** the new employees.

**BEWILDERED** *(adjective)* | হতবুদ্ধি, বিভ্রান্ত
Completely perplexed and confused; utterly puzzled.
✅ confused, perplexed, baffled, disoriented, puzzled, mystified, lost
❌ clear-headed, certain, composed, confident, oriented, sure
📝 The **bewildered** tourist stared at the map, unable to find the hotel.

**BEWITCH** *(verb)* | জাদু করা, মুগ্ধ করা
To cast a spell over; to enchant or fascinate someone completely.
✅ enchant, captivate, charm, fascinate, entrance, spellbind, mesmerize
❌ repel, disgust, bore, disenchant, free, release
📝 The performer **bewitched** the audience with her extraordinary voice.

**BILLOWING** *(adjective/verb)* | ফুলে ওঠা, ঢেউ খেলানো
Moving or swelling in large, rolling waves; puffing outward.
✅ swelling, surging, rolling, ballooning, puffing, undulating, flowing
❌ flat, still, deflated, calm, shrinking, contracted
📝 The **billowing** sails of the ship caught the strong ocean wind.

**BRUSQUE** *(adjective)* | রুক্ষ, অমায়িক, সংক্ষিপ্ত ও কঠোর
Abrupt or offhand in speech or manner; blunt without warmth.
✅ curt, abrupt, blunt, sharp, terse, gruff, short, rude
❌ polite, gentle, courteous, warm, friendly, tactful, diplomatic
📝 His **brusque** reply made the customer feel unwelcome at the store.

**CHAGRIN** *(noun)* | হতাশা, মনোকষ্ট, লজ্জাজনক অনুভূতি
Distress or embarrassment at having failed or been humiliated.
✅ disappointment, embarrassment, dismay, mortification, vexation, discomfort
❌ delight, satisfaction, pleasure, pride, happiness, joy
📝 To her **chagrin**, she arrived late and missed the award ceremony.

**CONVALESCE** *(verb)* | সুস্থ হওয়া, আরোগ্য লাভ করা
To recover one's health and strength over a period of time after illness.
✅ recover, recuperate, heal, mend, improve, get better, restore health
❌ deteriorate, worsen, relapse, decline, weaken, succumb
📝 He spent two weeks at home to **convalesce** after the surgery.

**CORTEGE** *(noun)* | শোভাযাত্রা, শবযাত্রা
A solemn procession, especially for a funeral.
✅ procession, funeral march, parade, retinue, train, column, motorcade
❌ celebration, festivity, party, jubilee, gathering
📝 The **cortege** moved slowly through the streets as mourners paid their respects.

**CURT** *(adjective)* | সংক্ষিপ্ত ও রুক্ষ, রূঢ়ভাবে সংক্ষিপ্ত
Rudely brief in speech or manner; abruptly dismissive.
✅ abrupt, brusque, terse, blunt, short, gruff, snappy
❌ polite, warm, expansive, courteous, verbose, elaborate, friendly
📝 Her **curt** response made it clear she was not interested in the conversation.

**DALLY** *(verb)* | সময় নষ্ট করা, গড়িমসি করা
To act or move slowly; to delay or waste time; to trifle.
✅ dawdle, loiter, linger, procrastinate, delay, waste time, dither
❌ hurry, rush, hasten, proceed, advance, be prompt
📝 Stop **dallying** and finish your homework before dinner.

**DECEIT** *(noun)* | প্রতারণা, ধোঁকা, মিথ্যাচার
The action or practice of deceiving someone; dishonesty.
✅ deception, fraud, trickery, dishonesty, duplicity, guile, falseness
❌ honesty, truthfulness, sincerity, integrity, frankness, candor
📝 The contract was full of **deceit**, designed to mislead investors.

**DECIPHER** *(verb)* | পাঠোদ্ধার করা, সংকেত বোঝা
To succeed in understanding, interpreting, or converting coded information.
✅ decode, interpret, unravel, translate, crack, solve, figure out
❌ encode, cipher, scramble, encrypt, conceal, obscure
📝 The archaeologist worked hard to **decipher** the ancient inscriptions.

**DELIRIOUS** *(adjective)* | প্রলাপী, উত্তেজিত, অত্যন্ত খুশি
In an acutely disturbed state of mind; wildly excited or ecstatic.
✅ frenzied, ecstatic, frantic, overjoyed, wild, euphoric, hysterical
❌ calm, composed, lucid, rational, subdued, sober, balanced
📝 The fans were **delirious** with joy when their team scored the final goal.

**DENIZEN** *(noun)* | অধিবাসী, বাসিন্দা
A person, animal, or plant that lives or is found in a particular place.
✅ inhabitant, resident, occupant, dweller, native, citizen, settler
❌ alien, stranger, visitor, outsider, foreigner, tourist
📝 The birds are **denizens** of the forest who rarely venture into the city.

**DEPRAVITY** *(noun)* | নৈতিক অধঃপতন, দুষ্টতা, পাপাচার
Moral corruption; the state of being wicked and immoral.
✅ wickedness, corruption, immorality, vice, sinfulness, vileness, degeneracy
❌ virtue, morality, purity, goodness, integrity, righteousness
📝 The **depravity** of the criminal's actions shocked the entire community.

**DEPRECATE** *(verb)* | নিন্দা করা, হেয় করা, মূল্যহ্রাস করা
To express disapproval of; to belittle or disparage something or someone.
✅ disparage, belittle, denounce, criticize, undervalue, discredit, censure
❌ praise, appreciate, commend, applaud, celebrate, endorse
📝 She was quick to **deprecate** her own achievements out of false modesty.

**DERISIVE** *(adjective)* | বিদ্রূপমূলক, তাচ্ছিল্যকর
Expressing contempt or ridicule; mocking and scornful.
✅ mocking, scornful, contemptuous, ridiculing, sneering, sarcastic, disdainful
❌ respectful, admiring, complimentary, praising, approving, sincere
📝 The audience gave a **derisive** laugh at the politician's empty promises.

**DISSECT** *(verb)* | ব্যবচ্ছেদ করা, বিশ্লেষণ করা
To cut open and examine; to analyze methodically and in detail.
✅ analyze, examine, scrutinize, investigate, break down, study, probe
❌ assemble, combine, ignore, overlook, neglect, integrate
📝 Students **dissect** frogs in biology class to learn about anatomy.

**DISSEMBLE** *(verb)* | ভান করা, আসল উদ্দেশ্য লুকানো
To conceal or disguise one's true feelings, motives, or character.
✅ pretend, feign, disguise, mask, camouflage, hide, deceive
❌ reveal, disclose, expose, be honest, show, express openly
📝 She tried to **dissemble** her anxiety with a forced smile.

**DIVEST** *(verb)* | বঞ্চিত করা, কেড়ে নেওয়া
To deprive someone of power, rights, or possessions; to strip of.
✅ deprive, strip, dispossess, remove, take away, disown, relieve
❌ invest, grant, give, bestow, provide, endow, supply
📝 The court ordered the company to **divest** its foreign assets.

**EFFRONT** *(noun)* | ধৃষ্টতা, বেহায়াপনা, স্পর্ধা
Bold and shameless audacity; insolent behavior or attitude.
✅ audacity, impudence, boldness, gall, nerve, insolence, brazenness
❌ modesty, timidity, shyness, humility, reserve, diffidence
📝 She had the **effront** to demand a refund after using the product for months.

**ELEGY** *(noun)* | শোক-কবিতা, বিলাপ-কাব্য
A mournful poem or song; a piece of writing that mourns the loss of something.
✅ lament, dirge, requiem, threnody, mournful poem, mourning verse
❌ celebration, ode of joy, hymn, anthem, eulogy (of praise)
📝 The poet wrote a moving **elegy** for his fallen comrades.

**ENRAGE** *(verb)* | ক্রুদ্ধ করা, রাগিয়ে তোলা
To make someone very angry; to fill with extreme fury.
✅ infuriate, incense, anger, madden, provoke, exasperate, inflame
❌ calm, soothe, pacify, appease, placate, mollify
📝 The unjust decision **enraged** the workers and led to a strike.

**EPICURE** *(noun)* | খাদ্যরসিক, ভোজনপ্রিয় ব্যক্তি
A person who takes particular pleasure in fine food and drink.
✅ gourmet, food connoisseur, gastronome, foodie, gourmand, bon vivant
❌ ascetic, abstainer, spartan, frugal person, dieter
📝 The **epicure** spent the evening sampling rare wines and artisan cheeses.

**EPITAPH** *(noun)* | সমাধিলিপি, কারো মৃত্যুর পরে লেখা স্মরণবাক্য
A phrase or statement written in memory of a person who has died.
✅ memorial inscription, tribute, commemoration, obituary, memorial verse
❌ birth announcement, greeting, introduction, welcome
📝 His **epitaph** read: "He gave his life so others might live."

**EQUIVOCAL** *(adjective)* | দ্ব্যর্থবোধক, অস্পষ্ট, অনিশ্চিত
Open to more than one interpretation; uncertain or unclear in meaning.
✅ ambiguous, unclear, vague, uncertain, dubious, indefinite, evasive
❌ clear, definite, certain, unambiguous, explicit, straightforward
📝 His **equivocal** answer left everyone unsure of his true intentions.

**ESOTERIC** *(adjective)* | অভ্যন্তরীণ, দুর্বোধ্য, সীমিত জনের জন্য
Intended for or understood by only a small group with special knowledge.
✅ obscure, arcane, cryptic, abstruse, mysterious, secret, specialized
❌ common, popular, widely known, mainstream, obvious, accessible
📝 The professor's lecture was so **esoteric** that only a few specialists understood it.

**EUPHEMISM** *(noun)* | মার্জিত বিকল্প শব্দ, লঘু ভাষা
A mild or indirect expression used instead of a blunt or harsh one.
✅ polite substitute, mild expression, indirect term, soft language, understatement
❌ bluntness, harsh expression, direct statement, vulgarity, profanity
📝 Saying "passed away" instead of "died" is a common **euphemism**.

**EXCULPATE** *(verb)* | দোষমুক্ত করা, নির্দোষ প্রমাণ করা
To show or declare that someone is not guilty of wrongdoing; to clear of blame.
✅ acquit, absolve, clear, vindicate, exonerate, pardon, free
❌ blame, condemn, accuse, convict, indict, charge, implicate
📝 The new evidence was enough to **exculpate** the wrongly accused man.

**EXPURGATE** *(verb)* | সংশোধন করা, আপত্তিকর অংশ বাদ দেওয়া
To remove objectionable or offensive material from a text; to censor and purge.
✅ censor, purge, sanitize, clean up, edit out, bowdlerize, remove
❌ include, add, restore, preserve, publish unedited, keep intact
📝 The publisher decided to **expurgate** certain scenes before releasing the novel.

**FATALIST** *(noun)* | ভাগ্যবাদী, নিয়তিতে বিশ্বাসী
A person who believes events are predetermined and inevitable; one who accepts fate.
✅ believer in fate, determinist, pessimist, resigned person, stoic
❌ optimist, idealist, activist, free-will believer, go-getter
📝 As a **fatalist**, he never worried about the future, believing everything was destined.

**GLIB** *(adjective)* | অগভীর, তৈলাক্ত কথাবার্তাকারী, মসৃণভাষী
Fluent and voluble but lacking depth or thought; slick and superficial.
✅ slick, smooth-talking, superficial, pat, facile, shallow, fluent but insincere
❌ sincere, deep, thoughtful, genuine, meaningful, heartfelt
📝 His **glib** response to the serious question disappointed the interviewer.

**GLOOM** *(noun)* | অন্ধকার, বিষণ্নতা, মনমরাভাব
Darkness; a feeling of sadness and despondency; melancholy atmosphere.
✅ darkness, sadness, melancholy, depression, bleakness, despair, dreariness
❌ brightness, joy, happiness, cheerfulness, delight, optimism
📝 A sense of **gloom** settled over the village after the devastating flood.

**GROSS** *(adjective)* | স্থূল, অতিরিক্ত, জঘন্য
Conspicuously obvious; extremely offensive or unpleasant; total before deductions.
✅ blatant, flagrant, obvious, coarse, crude, obscene, total, utter
❌ subtle, refined, net, partial, delicate, mild, sophisticated
📝 The auditors found a **gross** misappropriation of funds in the accounts.

**HASTY** *(adjective)* | তাড়াহুড়ো করা, অগোছালো, তড়িঘড়ি
Done with excessive speed or urgency; acting too quickly without care.
✅ hurried, rushed, impulsive, rash, careless, quick, precipitate
❌ careful, deliberate, cautious, thoughtful, measured, slow, considered
📝 His **hasty** decision without consulting the team led to costly mistakes.

**HEEDLESS** *(adjective)* | অমনোযোগী, অসতর্ক, পরোয়াহীন
Showing a reckless lack of care or attention; disregarding warnings.
✅ careless, reckless, inattentive, negligent, thoughtless, unmindful, oblivious
❌ careful, cautious, attentive, mindful, aware, vigilant, prudent
📝 **Heedless** of the danger signs, he continued driving through the storm.

**IMPLACABLE** *(adjective)* | অদম্য, অপ্রতিরোধ্য, যাকে থামানো যায় না
Unable to be appeased or placated; relentless in hostility or opposition.
✅ relentless, inexorable, unyielding, inflexible, unappeasable, merciless
❌ forgiving, flexible, yielding, merciful, accommodating, appeased
📝 The **implacable** enemy refused every peace offer without negotiation.

**INCENSE** *(verb)* | অত্যন্ত রাগিয়ে দেওয়া, ক্রুদ্ধ করা
To make someone extremely angry; to infuriate.
✅ enrage, infuriate, anger, madden, provoke, exasperate, inflame
❌ calm, pacify, soothe, appease, please, delight
📝 His dismissive attitude **incensed** everyone in the room.

**INJUDICIOUS** *(adjective)* | অবিবেচনাপ্রসূত, নির্বোধ, অজ্ঞ
Not having or showing good judgment; unwise.
✅ unwise, imprudent, foolish, ill-advised, rash, thoughtless, indiscreet
❌ wise, prudent, judicious, sensible, thoughtful, careful, sound
📝 It was **injudicious** to make such a bold claim without supporting evidence.

**INSCRUTABLE** *(adjective)* | দুর্বোধ্য, রহস্যময়, বোঝা কঠিন
Impossible to understand or interpret; mysterious and enigmatic.
✅ enigmatic, mysterious, unfathomable, cryptic, impenetrable, hidden
❌ obvious, transparent, clear, understandable, legible, readable
📝 His **inscrutable** expression gave no clue about what he was thinking.

**INSIPID** *(adjective)* | নীরস, স্বাদহীন, বিরক্তিকর
Lacking flavour; weak or tasteless; lacking vigor or interest.
✅ bland, tasteless, flavourless, dull, vapid, flat, uninteresting, boring
❌ tasty, flavorful, interesting, vibrant, exciting, stimulating, spicy
📝 The soup was so **insipid** that no amount of seasoning could save it.

**INSURGENT** *(noun/adjective)* | বিদ্রোহী, বিপ্লবী
A person who rises in active revolt; relating to rebellion against authority.
✅ rebel, revolutionary, mutineer, insurrectionist, dissident, renegade
❌ loyalist, conformist, supporter, defender, follower, conservative
📝 The government deployed troops to suppress the **insurgent** forces.

**JUBILATION** *(noun)* | আনন্দোচ্ছ্বাস, বিজয়োৎসব
A feeling of great happiness and triumph; celebration with great joy.
✅ triumph, joy, rejoicing, exultation, elation, celebration, festivity
❌ grief, sorrow, misery, lamentation, mourning, despair
📝 The streets were filled with **jubilation** after the championship victory.

**JUXTAPOSE** *(verb)* | পাশাপাশি রাখা, তুলনার জন্য স্থাপন করা
To place or deal with close together for contrasting effect.
✅ place side by side, compare, contrast, set side by side, position together
❌ separate, isolate, remove, distance, detach, oppose
📝 The artist **juxtaposed** bright colors and dark shadows to create contrast.

**LUDICROUS** *(adjective)* | হাস্যকর, উপহাসজনক, অযৌক্তিক
So foolish, unreasonable, or out of place as to be amusing; ridiculous.
✅ ridiculous, absurd, comical, farcical, laughable, preposterous, silly
❌ sensible, reasonable, logical, serious, credible, rational
📝 The idea of running a marathon without training seemed **ludicrous**.

**LUGUBRIOUS** *(adjective)* | অতিমাত্রায় দুঃখী, করুণ, বিষণ্ণ
Looking or sounding sad and dismal; mournful to an exaggerated degree.
✅ mournful, sorrowful, gloomy, melancholy, dismal, woeful, doleful
❌ cheerful, happy, joyful, lighthearted, merry, bright, optimistic
📝 He spoke in a **lugubrious** tone that made the ordinary story sound tragic.

**LUMINESCENT** *(adjective)* | আলোকজ্জ্বল, উজ্জ্বল, দীপ্তিমান
Emitting light not caused by heat; glowing with a soft, clear light.
✅ glowing, radiant, shining, bright, incandescent, luminous, gleaming
❌ dark, dull, dim, opaque, shadowy, murky, gloomy
📝 The **luminescent** jellyfish lit up the dark ocean with a gentle blue glow.

**LURID** *(adjective)* | ভয়ংকর, বীভৎস, উগ্র
Unpleasantly vivid in color; presented in a sensationally shocking or disturbing way.
✅ gruesome, shocking, graphic, garish, ghastly, vivid, sensational
❌ mild, tasteful, subdued, restrained, pleasant, gentle, calm
📝 The tabloid's **lurid** headline exaggerated the minor incident into a scandal.

**MANDATE** *(noun/verb)* | ম্যান্ডেট, আদেশ, অনুমোদন
An official order or commission; the authority to carry out a policy.
✅ order, command, directive, authorization, instruction, decree, sanction
❌ suggestion, request, appeal, option, choice, recommendation
📝 The voters gave the party a clear **mandate** to reform the healthcare system.

**METAMORPHOSIS** *(noun)* | রূপান্তর, আমূল পরিবর্তন
A complete change of form, structure, or substance; a transformation.
✅ transformation, change, conversion, evolution, alteration, transmutation
❌ stagnation, preservation, stability, sameness, constancy, retention
📝 The caterpillar's **metamorphosis** into a butterfly is a wonder of nature.

**MISCHIEVOUS** *(adjective)* | দুষ্টু, বদমাইশ, ক্ষতিকর উদ্দেশ্যপ্রণোদিত
Causing mischief; playfully annoying; intended to cause harm or trouble.
✅ naughty, playful, troublesome, roguish, impish, prankish, rascally
❌ well-behaved, obedient, serious, dutiful, good, angelic
📝 The **mischievous** child hid his sister's favorite toy just to annoy her.

**MODESTY** *(noun)* | বিনম্রতা, লজ্জাশীলতা, অহংকারহীনতা
The quality of not being too proud or confident about one's abilities; humility.
✅ humility, meekness, restraint, reserve, diffidence, unpretentiousness
❌ arrogance, conceit, pride, vanity, boastfulness, egotism
📝 Despite winning the award, she accepted it with great **modesty**.

**MOMENTOUS** *(adjective)* | অত্যন্ত গুরুত্বপূর্ণ, ঐতিহাসিক, সিদ্ধান্তমূলক
Of great importance or significance, especially in having a bearing on future events.
✅ significant, pivotal, crucial, vital, critical, weighty, consequential
❌ trivial, insignificant, unimportant, petty, minor, negligible
📝 The signing of the treaty was a **momentous** event in the nation's history.

**MULL** *(verb)* | গভীরভাবে ভাবা, চিন্তা করা
To think about something carefully and at length; to ponder or consider.
✅ ponder, contemplate, consider, reflect, deliberate, meditate, think over
❌ ignore, dismiss, overlook, rush, decide impulsively, neglect
📝 She needed time to **mull** over the job offer before giving her answer.

**MUNIFICENT** *(adjective)* | অত্যন্ত উদার, দানশীল, মহানুভব
More generous than is usual or necessary; lavishly giving.
✅ generous, bountiful, lavish, magnanimous, charitable, liberal, open-handed
❌ stingy, miserly, mean, tight-fisted, frugal, ungenerous
📝 The **munificent** donor funded an entire wing of the new hospital.

**MUTATION** *(noun)* | পরিবর্তন, রূপান্তর, পরিব্যক্তি
The process of change; a distinct form resulting from such a change.
✅ transformation, alteration, change, variation, evolution, modification
❌ stability, constancy, preservation, permanence, sameness, stasis
📝 Scientists are studying the **mutation** of the virus to develop a vaccine.

**MUTINOUS** *(adjective)* | বিদ্রোহী, অবাধ্য, আদেশ না মানা
Willing to mutiny; refusing to obey authority; rebellious.
✅ rebellious, defiant, insubordinate, insurgent, revolting, seditious
❌ obedient, loyal, compliant, faithful, dutiful, submissive
📝 The **mutinous** sailors seized control of the ship from the captain.

**NAÏVE** *(adjective)* | সরল, অনভিজ্ঞ, ভোলাভালা
Showing a lack of experience, wisdom, or judgment; innocently simple.
✅ innocent, guileless, unsophisticated, trusting, gullible, unworldly, simple
❌ worldly, shrewd, experienced, sophisticated, cunning, cynical
📝 It was **naïve** to believe that success would come without hard work.

**OBSERVANT** *(adjective)* | মনোযোগী, সতর্ক, খেয়ালী
Quick to notice things; paying close attention; alert and watchful.
✅ attentive, alert, watchful, perceptive, sharp, vigilant, keen, mindful
❌ inattentive, unaware, careless, oblivious, heedless, blind
📝 The **observant** detective noticed the small scratch on the doorframe.

**PERPETUATION** *(noun)* | অব্যাহত রাখা, অনন্তকাল চালিয়ে যাওয়া
The action of preserving something or causing it to continue indefinitely.
✅ continuation, preservation, maintenance, sustenance, immortalization, prolonging
❌ cessation, end, termination, conclusion, abolition, discontinuation
📝 The **perpetuation** of harmful stereotypes can damage entire communities.

**PIQUANT** *(adjective)* | ঝাঁঝালো, উত্তেজনাপূর্ণ, চটপটে
Having a pleasantly sharp, tart, or spicy flavor; pleasantly stimulating to the mind.
✅ spicy, tangy, zesty, sharp, pungent, lively, stimulating, interesting
❌ bland, insipid, tasteless, dull, boring, flat, uninteresting
📝 The chef added lime and chili to give the sauce a **piquant** kick.

**PIVOTAL** *(adjective)* | কেন্দ্রীয়, অত্যন্ত গুরুত্বপূর্ণ, মূল
Of crucial importance in relation to the development or success of something.
✅ crucial, central, critical, key, vital, essential, decisive, significant
❌ trivial, minor, peripheral, unimportant, marginal, insignificant
📝 His **pivotal** role in the negotiations secured the historic peace deal.

**PLAGIARISM** *(noun)* | সাহিত্যচৌর্য, অন্যের লেখা নিজের বলে দাবি করা
The practice of taking someone else's work or ideas and passing them off as one's own.
✅ copying, theft, piracy, appropriation, cheating, fraud, intellectual theft
❌ originality, creativity, acknowledgment, citation, attribution
📝 The student was expelled for **plagiarism** after submitting a copied essay.

**PONDER** *(verb)* | গভীরভাবে ভাবা, মনে মনে বিবেচনা করা
To think about something carefully, especially before making a decision.
✅ contemplate, consider, reflect, deliberate, meditate, ruminate, mull over
❌ ignore, dismiss, act impulsively, rush, overlook, decide hastily
📝 He **pondered** the question for a long moment before answering.

**PREVARICATE** *(verb)* | সত্য এড়িয়ে যাওয়া, কথা ঘোরানো
To speak or act in an evasive way; to avoid giving a direct answer.
✅ equivocate, evade, hedge, dodge, mislead, beat around the bush, stall
❌ be direct, speak honestly, state plainly, tell the truth, be candid
📝 When asked about the budget, the minister began to **prevaricate** uncomfortably.

**RANCOR** *(noun)* | বিদ্বেষ, দীর্ঘস্থায়ী ক্রোধ, পুরনো ক্ষোভ
Bitterness or resentfulness, especially when long-standing; deep hatred.
✅ bitterness, resentment, spite, animosity, hostility, malice, grudge
❌ goodwill, forgiveness, kindness, affection, warmth, friendliness
📝 Even years after the dispute, he still spoke of his rival with deep **rancor**.

**RAVAGE** *(verb/noun)* | ধ্বংস করা, লুণ্ঠন করা, ক্ষতিসাধন করা
To cause severe and extensive damage; to lay waste to something.
✅ devastate, destroy, ruin, plunder, pillage, wreak havoc, demolish
❌ restore, repair, protect, preserve, build, nurture, save
📝 The wildfire **ravaged** thousands of acres of pristine forest in just days.

**RECOMPENSE** *(noun/verb)* | ক্ষতিপূরণ, পুরস্কার, প্রতিদান দেওয়া
Compensation or reward given for loss, suffering, or effort; to make amends.
✅ compensation, reward, reparation, repayment, remuneration, amends
❌ punishment, deprivation, penalty, loss, forfeiture, withholding
📝 The court ordered the company to provide **recompense** for the workers' injuries.

**RECUPERATE** *(verb)* | সুস্থ হওয়া, শক্তি ফিরে পাওয়া
To recover from illness or tiredness; to regain health, strength, or well-being.
✅ recover, convalesce, heal, mend, get better, restore, bounce back
❌ deteriorate, worsen, collapse, relapse, decline, weaken
📝 She took a week off work to **recuperate** after her knee surgery.

**REFURBISH** *(verb)* | সংস্কার করা, নতুন করা, পুনরুজ্জীবিত করা
To renovate and redecorate; to restore to good condition.
✅ renovate, restore, renew, revamp, repair, redecorate, overhaul
❌ neglect, damage, destroy, deteriorate, abandon, wreck
📝 The hotel was closed for months to **refurbish** all its rooms and facilities.

**RESCIND** *(verb)* | বাতিল করা, প্রত্যাহার করা, রদ করা
To revoke, cancel, or repeal a law, order, or agreement; to take back officially.
✅ revoke, cancel, repeal, annul, retract, withdraw, abolish, nullify
❌ enforce, uphold, maintain, confirm, establish, implement, ratify
📝 The government decided to **rescind** the controversial tax law after public protests.

**RESENTMENT** *(noun)* | বিরক্তি, ক্ষোভ, অসন্তোষ
Bitter indignation at having been treated unfairly; a feeling of anger and displeasure.
✅ bitterness, animosity, grudge, rancor, hostility, indignation, displeasure
❌ forgiveness, contentment, goodwill, acceptance, gratitude, happiness
📝 Her years of **resentment** over the unfair promotion finally came to a boil.

**RESTRICTIVE** *(adjective)* | সীমাবদ্ধকারী, নিয়ন্ত্রণমূলক
Imposing restrictions; limiting freedom or activity.
✅ limiting, constraining, confining, prohibitive, controlling, tight, binding
❌ permissive, liberal, free, open, flexible, lenient, unrestricted
📝 The new **restrictive** trade policies affected dozens of small businesses.

**SAGACIOUS** *(adjective)* | বুদ্ধিমান, দূরদর্শী, প্রজ্ঞাশালী
Having or showing keen mental discernment and good judgment; wise.
✅ wise, shrewd, astute, perceptive, insightful, discerning, judicious
❌ foolish, naive, dull, unwise, short-sighted, ignorant, reckless
📝 The **sagacious** elder gave advice that guided the village through difficult times.

**SCOFF** *(verb)* | উপহাস করা, তাচ্ছিল্য করা
To speak to someone or about something in a scornfully derisive or mocking way.
✅ mock, ridicule, sneer, jeer, deride, taunt, belittle, dismiss
❌ praise, admire, respect, commend, applaud, take seriously
📝 They **scoffed** at his idea, not realizing it would later become a success.

**SENILE** *(adjective)* | বার্ধক্যজনিত, বয়সের কারণে দুর্বল
Having or showing the weaknesses or diseases of old age, especially a loss of mental faculties.
✅ aged, decrepit, infirm, doddering, frail, ancient, mentally impaired
❌ youthful, vigorous, alert, sharp, energetic, fresh, young
📝 The old professor's **senile** forgetfulness saddened his former students.

**SEVERE** *(adjective)* | কঠোর, তীব্র, গুরুতর
Very great; intense; harsh or strict; causing hardship or discomfort.
✅ harsh, intense, strict, extreme, serious, grave, rigorous, stern
❌ mild, gentle, lenient, light, moderate, easy, trivial
📝 The region suffered **severe** drought conditions for the third year in a row.

**SHIMMER** *(verb/noun)* | মৃদুভাবে চকচক করা, ঝলমল করা
To shine with a soft, slightly wavering light; a soft, slightly wavering light.
✅ glimmer, sparkle, glisten, gleam, twinkle, flicker, glow, glint
❌ dull, darken, fade, dim, shadow, blacken
📝 The lake **shimmered** in the early morning sunlight like polished silver.

**STIMULATE** *(verb)* | উদ্দীপিত করা, উৎসাহিত করা
To raise levels of physiological or nervous activity; to encourage interest or activity.
✅ energize, invigorate, excite, inspire, activate, provoke, galvanize, motivate
❌ depress, suppress, calm, discourage, dull, inhibit, sedate
📝 The teacher used creative games to **stimulate** the students' curiosity.

**SUBMISSIVE** *(adjective)* | বাধ্য, অনুগত, নতিস্বীকারকারী
Ready to conform to the authority or will of others; meekly obedient.
✅ obedient, compliant, docile, yielding, meek, acquiescent, passive
❌ dominant, assertive, defiant, aggressive, rebellious, disobedient
📝 The overly **submissive** employee never stood up for his own rights.

**SUPERFLUOUS** *(adjective)* | অপ্রয়োজনীয়, বাহুল্যপূর্ণ, অতিরিক্ত
Unnecessary, especially through being more than enough; redundant.
✅ unnecessary, redundant, excessive, surplus, extra, needless, wasteful
❌ necessary, essential, needed, vital, required, indispensable
📝 The report was full of **superfluous** details that distracted from the main point.

**SUPERIMPOSE** *(verb)* | একটির উপর আরেকটি রাখা, আরোপ করা
To place or lay one thing over another, especially so both are evident.
✅ overlay, place over, layer, impose on top, project over, juxtapose
❌ separate, remove, detach, divide, isolate, pull apart
📝 The editor **superimposed** a subtitle over the original film footage.

**SUPERSEDE** *(verb)* | প্রতিস্থাপন করা, স্থান নেওয়া
To take the place of a person or thing previously in authority or use; to replace.
✅ replace, displace, succeed, supplant, override, oust, take over from
❌ maintain, keep, retain, preserve, uphold, support, reinforce
📝 The new regulation will **supersede** all previous rules on data privacy.

**SYNCHRONIZE** *(verb)* | একসাথে মেলানো, সমন্বয় করা
To cause to occur or operate at the same time or rate; to coordinate.
✅ coordinate, harmonize, align, match, calibrate, time together, integrate
❌ desynchronize, misalign, disconnect, clash, conflict, diverge
📝 The dancers **synchronized** their movements perfectly to the music.

**TATTER** *(noun/verb)* | কাপড়ের টুকরো, ছিন্নভিন্ন অবস্থা
A torn, ragged piece of cloth; to tear into irregular pieces.
✅ shred, rag, scrap, fragment, remnant, piece, strip
❌ whole, intact cloth, complete, undamaged, pristine
📝 His coat was in **tatters** after months of rough travel through the wilderness.

**TIMIDITY** *(noun)* | ভীরুতা, সংকোচ, সাহসহীনতা
Lack of courage or confidence; the quality of being nervous and easily frightened.
✅ shyness, fearfulness, cowardice, meekness, diffidence, nervousness, hesitation
❌ boldness, courage, confidence, bravery, audacity, daring
📝 His **timidity** prevented him from speaking up during the important meeting.

**TRANQUIL** *(adjective)* | শান্ত, নিস্তরঙ্গ, প্রশান্ত
Free from disturbance; calm and peaceful; quiet and undisturbed.
✅ calm, peaceful, serene, quiet, still, placid, undisturbed, restful
❌ turbulent, agitated, stormy, noisy, restless, chaotic, disturbed
📝 She found the **tranquil** countryside a welcome escape from city life.

**TRANQUILIZE** *(verb)* | শান্ত করা, নিস্তেজ করা
To make calm and quiet; to sedate using a tranquilizing substance.
✅ calm, sedate, pacify, soothe, quieten, settle, relax, subdue
❌ excite, agitate, disturb, stimulate, provoke, arouse, irritate
📝 The vet used a dart to **tranquilize** the escaped lion before relocating it.

**UBIQUITY** *(noun)* | সর্বত্র উপস্থিতি, সর্বব্যাপিতা
The fact of appearing everywhere or of being very widespread.
✅ omnipresence, pervasiveness, universality, prevalence, widespread presence
❌ rarity, absence, scarcity, obscurity, limited presence
📝 The **ubiquity** of smartphones has transformed how people communicate globally.

**UNOBTRUSIVE** *(adjective)* | অনুল্লেখযোগ্য, নজরে না পড়া, বিনয়ী
Not conspicuous or attracting attention; modest and restrained.
✅ inconspicuous, subtle, modest, understated, quiet, low-key, discreet
❌ conspicuous, bold, showy, prominent, loud, attention-grabbing
📝 The **unobtrusive** security cameras blended seamlessly into the decor.

**UPROAR** *(noun)* | হইচই, কোলাহল, তোলপাড়
A loud and impassioned noise or disturbance; a state of commotion.
✅ commotion, tumult, chaos, disturbance, noise, clamor, pandemonium
❌ silence, calm, quiet, tranquility, peace, serenity, order
📝 The announcement caused an **uproar** among the students in the auditorium.

**VERACIOUS** *(adjective)* | সত্যবাদী, সত্যনিষ্ঠ
Telling the truth; habitually speaking the truth; accurate and honest.
✅ truthful, honest, accurate, trustworthy, sincere, candid, reliable
❌ lying, dishonest, deceptive, false, misleading, untruthful
📝 A journalist must be **veracious** and verify every claim before publishing.

**VERACITY** *(noun)* | সত্যবাদিতা, সত্যতা, সত্যনিষ্ঠা
Conformity to facts; accuracy; the quality of being true or accurate.
✅ truthfulness, accuracy, honesty, reliability, correctness, authenticity
❌ falsehood, deception, dishonesty, inaccuracy, lying, untruth
📝 The **veracity** of the witness's account was confirmed by security footage.

**VIVID** *(adjective)* | প্রাণবন্ত, উজ্জ্বল, স্পষ্ট
Producing powerful feelings or strong, clear images in the mind; bright and striking.
✅ bright, intense, striking, colorful, brilliant, clear, lifelike, expressive
❌ dull, faded, dim, lifeless, pale, muted, vague, colorless
📝 She has a **vivid** imagination that brings her stories to life.

**VOLUBLE** *(adjective)* | বাচাল, অনর্গল কথাবলা, সপ্রতিভ
Talking fluently, readily, and at length; talkative; using many words.
✅ talkative, loquacious, garrulous, verbose, chatty, fluent, articulate
❌ silent, taciturn, reserved, reticent, quiet, restrained, laconic
📝 The **voluble** tour guide kept the group entertained throughout the trip.
